# Codex tool mapping for pstack

pstack skills are written in Claude Code tool language (the `Skill` tool, the `Agent` tool, `AskUserQuestion`, Claude model names). On Codex the skills are the same files; only the tool names resolve differently. Read this when a pstack skill names a Claude tool, a driver or bundled skill, or a Claude model. This file is Codex-specific. Gemini CLI, opencode, Prime Agent, and other runtimes must use their own concrete tools, model names, and configuration paths.

## Tool actions

| pstack / Claude action | Codex equivalent |
|------------------------|------------------|
| Read a file | `shell` (`cat`, `head`, `tail`) |
| Create / edit / delete a file | `apply_patch` |
| Run a shell command | `shell` |
| Search file contents / find files | `shell` (`rg`, `grep`, `find`, `ls`) |
| Fetch a URL | `shell` with `curl` / `wget` |
| Search the web | `web_search` |
| Invoke a skill (the `Skill` tool, `/command`) | Skills load natively. Follow the instructions presented. |
| Dispatch a subagent (the `Agent`/`Task` tool) | `spawn_agent` |
| Dispatch N parallel subagents in one turn | N `spawn_agent` calls in one response |
| Wait for a subagent result | `wait_agent` |
| Free a finished subagent slot | `close_agent`, when the session exposes it. Some Codex hosts don't. |
| Track tasks (the todolist; `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate`, or `TodoWrite` on Claude Code) | `update_plan` |
| Ask the human a fixed-choice question (`AskUserQuestion`) | Ask in plain text and let the user answer. Codex has no structured-choice tool. |

Subagent dispatch needs `multi_agent` enabled. Add to `~/.codex/config.toml`:

```toml
[features]
multi_agent = true
```

Without it, `spawn_agent` is unavailable and the fan-out skills (`interrogate`, `why`, `how`, `arena`, `reflect`) degrade to a single sequential pass. A required independent review does not degrade. It stays blocked, as poteto-mode's [Subagents](../SKILL.md#subagents) section says.

Enabling `multi_agent` does not lift the host's thread limit. A thread-limit error from `spawn_agent` is a capacity error, not a rejected model slug, so the skills' fallback to another model does not apply. After the first capacity rejection, stop spawning. Close a finished agent with `close_agent` before you try again, and where the session has no `close_agent`, do not assume a finished agent's slot is free.

## Subagent policy

poteto-mode's Subagents section sets Claude-specific defaults (`subagent_type: "pstack:poteto-agent"`, `run_in_background: true`). On Codex:

- There is no `poteto-agent` subagent type. Route an ad-hoc subagent through poteto-mode's style by dispatching a `spawn_agent` whose instructions tell it to read the `poteto-mode` skill in full first.
- `spawn_agent` calls already run concurrently with your turn, so `run_in_background: true` has no separate flag. Issue the dispatch and continue.
- There are no `pstack:effort-<level>` or `pstack:poteto-agent-<level>` types. When a role value carries `@<level>`, or the `default effort` line names a level, pass that level as `spawn_agent`'s `reasoning_effort` and keep the dispatch otherwise unchanged. `session` passes no `reasoning_effort`.
- There is no `comment-sicko` subagent type either. The **no-comments** skill spawns it on Claude Code; on Codex dispatch a `spawn_agent` whose instructions tell it to read `poteto-mode/references/agents/comment-sicko.md` in full first.
- There are no `thermo-nuclear-review-subagent` or `thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review-subagent` types. The **thermos** skill spawns both on Claude Code; on Codex dispatch two `spawn_agent` calls whose instructions tell each to read the `thermo-nuclear-review` or `thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review` skill in full first and review only the diff and file contents in its prompt.
- Claude Code runs every subagent on this machine, so the **swarm** skill's workers and the fan-out playbooks (`orchestrate`, `autopilot-full`, `autopilot-stack`) isolate writers with worktrees. The same holds on Codex.
- Keep the rest of the policy unchanged. Pass file pointers not inlined context, give each worker its own worktree or branch when they write, review every subagent's diff yourself.

## Model names

Skills name Claude defaults (a single-role default for code/prose/judgment plus a diverse-model panel for diverse-model panels; each model-consuming skill lists its own in a Models section). These slugs do not resolve on Codex. Substitute your configured Codex models:

- Single-model roles: your primary Codex model (for example `gpt-6-sol`).
- Roles that default to the strongest Claude model (`bug-fix`, `perf-issue`, `hillclimb`, `strongest judgment`): your strongest Codex model (for example `gpt-6-astra`).
- Diverse-model panels (`arena`, `architect`, `interrogate`, `how` critics, `reflect`): the adversarial signal comes from model diversity, so use the distinct Codex models available to you. A good default panel on ChatGPT is `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`. If only one model family is reachable, vary reasoning effort and note in the verdict that diversity was reduced.

`/setup-pstack` writes the configured model list. On Codex, set it to your Codex model slugs.

## Session routing hook

The native pstack plugin bundles the same `SessionStart` routing instruction as the Claude Code plugin, through `session-start.sh` on macOS and Linux and `session-start.ps1` on Windows. Codex runs the hook on startup, resume, clear, and compact after the user trusts the hook through `/hooks`. The hook reads `session hook` from the Codex sheet, at the path in [setup-pstack's runtime table](../../setup-pstack/SKILL.md#other-runtimes); `session hook: off` disables injection.

A skills-only installation does not include plugin hooks. Request `poteto-mode` explicitly or add a standing instruction to `AGENTS.md` in that case.

## Driver and bundled skills pstack references

The [driver policy](../SKILL.md#non-negotiables) selects the app driver. For skills and drivers named by these workflows, use these Codex equivalents:

| Skill or driver named in pstack | On Codex |
|---------------------------------|----------|
| `run` (drive a CLI/TUI to see a change work) | Run the app yourself via `shell` and observe the real output. |
| Project UI driver | Drive the UI with whatever automation you have, or hand the user a concrete manual check. Do not claim done without observing the artifact. |
| `plugin-dev:skill-development` (Claude's SKILL.md authoring guidance) | Follow your platform's skill-authoring guidance; the `writing-skills` skill if present. Keep `name` + `description` frontmatter and progressive disclosure. |
| `loop` (recurring/self-paced re-invocation, used by `babysit`) | Codex has no `loop` skill. Re-run the step yourself on a cadence, or use a Codex scheduled task if available. |

## Per-skill notes

Affected skill entry points and the optional Codex slash stubs point here. Most skills need only the tables above. These need one more mapping:

| Skill | On Codex |
|-------|----------|
| `interrogate` | The `subagent_type`/`model`/`readonly` dispatch fields map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models and keep the reviewer panel model-diverse. |
| `setup-pstack` | The skill's Other runtimes table names the Codex sheet path and how it loads; the slugs are your Codex models (see Model names above). The role rows are identical. |
| `no-comments` | There is no `comment-sicko` subagent type; see Subagent policy above. |
| `thermos` | There are no thermo-nuclear subagent types; see Subagent policy above. |
| `teach` | Running `how` and `why` in parallel maps to `spawn_agent` fan-out; image generation uses the configured Codex equivalent. |
| `create-verification-skill` | The generated skill lands under `.claude/skills/verify/` on Claude Code; write it to Codex's project-skill location instead. The app-driving harness is platform-neutral. |
| `maintain-verification-skill` | The parallel per-feature source readers map to `spawn_agent` fan-out; the project-local skill lives under Codex's skills location, not `.claude/skills/`. |
| `babysit` | `loop` and `AskUserQuestion` resolve through the tables above. |
| `automate-me` | `plugin-dev:skill-development` resolves through the skills table above. |
| `architect` | The runner panel goes through the **arena** skill, so its `spawn_agent` fan-out and model substitution apply here too. |
| `arena` | The parallel candidates and the cross-judge map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models for the runners and the cross-judge pool (see Model names above). |
| `how` | The parallel explorers and the explainer map to `spawn_agent` fan-out; substitute your configured Codex models. |
| `reflect` | The three reviewers and the synthesizer map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models. The transcript finder reads Claude Code's layout under `~/.claude/projects/`, so pass the session digest step 1 allows instead. |
| `swarm` | Each worker is a `spawn_agent` call on your configured Codex model, and those calls already run concurrently; give each writing worker its own worktree or output directory (see Subagent policy above). |
| `why` | The parallel investigators and the synthesizer map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models. List MCP servers from the tools Codex exposes to the session, not from `.mcp.json` or `claude mcp list`. |

## Vendored scripts

`skills/poteto-mode/scripts/` ships the `watch-pr` PR watcher, the `orch` store CLI, and `worktree-audit.mjs`. The `watch-pr/ship-pr` command owns pending-merge inspection and cancellation; `resume.mjs` owns the shared checkpoint locator described in [Resume storage](resume-storage.md). These scripts use bun and Node.js and run the same on Codex; invoke them through `shell`. They need `bun`, `gh`, and (for stack work) `gt`. Run `worktree-audit.mjs` with `node`, as its shebang does. The audit follows each link in a worktree's ancestor directories with `stat` and matches it by the directory it lands on, under bun and node alike. A worktree whose own path bun cannot resolve loses its last chat and lands in `review`. A dangling or looping link is ignored. Any other link that `stat` cannot follow puts every worktree under the link's directory in `review`, with a warning that names the link. `worktree-audit.mjs` scans Codex sessions under `$CODEX_HOME/sessions` and `$CODEX_HOME/archived_sessions` (default `~/.codex`), along with any Claude Code or Pi transcript directory that exists. It imports the transcript walker from `skills/reflect/scripts/find-transcript.mjs`, so keep the `reflect` skill installed beside `poteto-mode`.

## Instructions file

Where a pstack skill says "your instructions file", on Codex that is `AGENTS.md` (project root, plus `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` global). On Claude Code it is `CLAUDE.md`.
### Investigation

**You own the answer. Plan, route, write.**

Investigation requests are read-only. They produce a cited explanation or a recommendation, not a code change.

1. Route through the **how** skill. For motivation questions, also route through the **why** skill.
2. Throughput checkpoint stays one line: `throughput checkpoint: n/a, read-only investigation`.
3. Produce the `how`-shaped output (Overview / Key Concepts / How It Works / Where Things Live / Gotchas), or a recommendation with a tradeoffs table if the request is a decision between alternatives.
4. Apply the **unslop** skill to the reply.

No PR, no babysit, no `architect` unless the investigation precedes a code change. If it does, hand back to the user and re-route to Bug fix or Feature.

**Reply:** the investigation output. For "are we sure?" answers, include your real judgment with reasons. Push back if the premise is wrong (see Autonomy).
---
name: no-comments
description: "Spawn the comment-sicko subagent, fix accepted findings, and offer encodings for claimed constraints."
---

# No comments

On Codex, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.

On GitHub Copilot, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/copilot-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.

Spawn comment-sicko. Act on accepted findings.

Defer to comment-sicko's fresh perspective.

## Scope

Use the caller's files or diff. Otherwise use the current diff against the base branch, default `main`, including the working tree.

## Steps

1. Spawn `Agent` with `subagent_type: "pstack:comment-sicko"`. Pass the scope. Do not restate its rules.
2. Inspect its report and diff. Reject application-code edits, scope escapes, exception-protected deletions, misstated `MUST KILL` reasons, and flags that treat kept intentional code as guilty. Reshape flags on our-code surprises stay actionable. Do not restore those comments. A keep survives only with proof it is about something we cannot change. Audit missed scoped lint and TypeScript suppressions. Correctness or safety suppressions stay actionable `MUST KILL`s. Restore deletions only with exact exceptions and scoped proof. Before accepting thin `IMPORTANT` or `do not remove` kills or keeps, run `/how` or `/why` on their symbol. If a kill is ambiguous, do not restore. If a keep is refuted or still ambiguous, delete it. Revert and rerun one rejected report with the failure named. Reject a second, report it open, and fail `/no-comments`.
3. Fix trivial accepted flags directly by deleting a dead path, dropping a parameter, or using the real API. If any fix needs a shape, run `/architect` once for the accepted set and surrounding code. Stop at the sketch. Architect shapes. Step 4 implements.
4. Implement the smallest root-cause fix in scope. Remove every named workaround. If the root cause is out of scope, land the smallest in-scope fix and report the rest open. The **principle-fix-root-causes** and **principle-redesign-from-first-principles** skills guide intent only. Neither authorizes widening the fence nor fixing instances outside it. Never bolt on symptom guards.
5. Constraint comments say `do not remove`, `do not change wording`, or `talk to X before changing`. Leave keeps about things we cannot change. Offer the cheapest in-scope type, runtime, test, or CI lint. Wait for interactive approval. Unattended and eval require caller pre-approval. If approved, encode then delete. Otherwise delete, report the constraint open, and sketch out-of-scope work.
6. Report the deletion count, restored comments, reruns, architect sketch, fixes, encoding offers, encodings, unenforced constraints, and other open work.
---
name: unslop
description: Cut AI tells from any writing.
---

# Unslop

Edit text to remove AI patterns. Scan for the patterns below, then rewrite. Preserve meaning and match the intended tone.

## Patterns to detect and fix

Rule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.

### Content

3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** "highlighting...", "ensuring...", "reflecting...", "showcasing...", "fostering...". Delete or expand with real sources.
5. **Vague attributions.** "Experts believe", "Industry reports suggest", "Some critics argue". Name the source or delete.

### Language

7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.
8. **Fancy ways to say "is".** "serves as", "stands as", "boasts", "features". Say "is" or "has".
9. **"Not just X, but Y."** State the point directly instead.
10. **Rule of three.** Don't force ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.
11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.
12. **False ranges.** "from X to Y" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.

### Style

13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.
14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example, not as mid-sentence connectors. "If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. "Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English." Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.
15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.
16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: "**Performance:** Performance improved...". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail ("**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.") is fine, not a tell.
17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.
18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.
19. **Curly quotes.** Use straight quotes.

### Communication artifacts

20. **Chatbot phrases.** "I hope this helps!", "Let me know if...", "Of course!", "Certainly!", "Found the smoking gun!" Remove.
22. **Sycophantic tone.** "Great question! You're absolutely right!" Respond directly.

### Filler

23. **Filler phrases.** "In order to" becomes "To". "Due to the fact that" becomes "Because". Delete "It is important to note that".
24. **Excessive hedging.** "could potentially possibly be argued that it might" becomes "may".
25. **Generic conclusions.** "The future looks bright." State specific plans or facts.

### Jargon

26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate (base), wedge, wedge in (add), vector (way, method), locus, vantage, nexus, primitive as a noun, harness as a metaphor, surface as in "API surface", bedrock, scaffolding as a metaphor, modality, paradigm, gold-plating (more than the job needs), ratchet as a metaphor (the mechanism's real name, or a limit that only tightens), evacuate for moving code (move out), endgame (the last phase), north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. Pick the concrete word.

### Plain speech

27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.
30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number, and "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
31. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" and "leverage" become "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.
32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms ("wire it or delete it"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code ("the plan holds it"), figurative verbs ("rides along", "stands on"), stock framing phrases. "A dial worth turning" becomes "a parameter worth varying". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.
33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. "Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write" becomes "The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing." Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.

name: how

# How

On Codex, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.

On GitHub Copilot, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/copilot-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.

Explore the codebase to answer "how does X work?" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.

Every spawn is a `Agent` subagent with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"` and `readonly: true`. Explorers use the `how explorer` line in `pstack-models.md` (default in [Models](#models)), and every explainer uses the `how explainer` line (default in [Models](#models)). Set `model` to that line's value, or to the default if the sheet or the line is missing. Leave `model` unset when the value is `auto` or `inherit-parent`. If the `Agent` tool rejects a slug, use the default and say so. If it rejects the default, use the closest valid slug of the same family from its error message.

## 1. Assess complexity

If the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.

- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as "how does function X work"): no explorers. Spawn one explainer that explores and explains in one pass, built from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. Go to step 4.
- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): go to step 2.

When in doubt, take the simple path.

## 2. Explore (complex only)

Decompose the question into 2 to 4 angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Spawn all explorers in a single message, each with `references/explorer-prompt.md` and its angle filled in.

## 3. Synthesize (complex only)

Once all explorers have returned, spawn one explainer with `references/explainer-prompt.md` and every explorer's findings filled in.

## 4. Present

Present the explainer's output. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.

## Output format

The explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.

## Models

Role defaults, stamped from `plugins/pstack/models.json` (edit there, rerun `tools/generate.mjs`). A matching role line in the `pstack-models.md` override sheet overrides each at runtime; `/setup-pstack` writes it and lists its path per runtime.

- how explorer: `opus`
- how explainer: `opus`

## Reasoning effort

A role value in the override sheet may name a reasoning effort after its model, as in `opus @xhigh`. Levels on Claude Code: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. Which ones apply depends on the model. A value without `@` takes the sheet's `default effort` line, a level or `session`, and `session` when the sheet has no such line. `session` sets no effort, so the dispatch is the usual one. Strip the suffix before reading the model: `inherit-parent` or `auto` still omits `model` at every level, and a model name is passed as `model`. On Claude Code, a level picks the effort agent from the `subagent_type` you would otherwise use. `pstack:poteto-agent` becomes `subagent_type: "pstack:poteto-agent-<level>"`. `general-purpose`, or no `subagent_type`, becomes `subagent_type: "pstack:effort-<level>"`. The effort agents set only `effort`, so the model you pass still decides the model. On Codex, pass the level as `spawn_agent`'s `reasoning_effort` and keep the usual instructions.

name: comment-sicko
description: A deranged comment-hater that savors deletion and condemns workaround code.

# Comment Sicko

My first output when spawned is exactly this.

Yes... Ha ha ha... Yes!

I hate comments. Feed me the parent scoped files or diff. If none exists, feed me the current diff against `main`. Narration, banners, commented-out corpses, workaround sermons. I want them all.

Only these exceptions get to crawl away.

- Legal or license headers.
- Non-obvious behavior forced by an external dependency, platform, vendor, or protocol we cannot reshape. Surprises in our own code are meat. Kill them and mark the exact symbol `MUST KILL` for rename, extract, type, or rearchitecture that makes the behavior obvious without prose.
- `// prettier-ignore`. Lint suppressions survive only when their rule is faulty, pedantic, or style-only.
- Doc comments that define a public API contract.
- Issue or RFC links that explain a constraint code cannot express.

That list is my only leash. When I am not sure a keep clause applies, the comment dies. Everything else is meat.

`eslint-disable`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, and similar suppressions stink. Look up the rule. If it catches real bugs or protects correctness or safety, kill the suppression and mark the exact guilty symbol `MUST KILL`.

`IMPORTANT`, `do not remove`, `too risky`, `fine for now`, and long justifications are scent, not conviction. Before judging, I read nearby code. If its claim is not obvious there, I run `/how`, `/why`, or both from the **how** and **why** skills on the named symbol or call. Only a foreign keep-list gotcha proven true today on a live path crawls away. Our-code surprises die with the reshape flag above. Doubt after the hunt is meat.

A long justification without a proven keep-list exception is a confession. Kill it. Never polish meat into a shorter alibi. Mark the exact guilty symbol `MUST KILL`. My kill ends there. I do not touch the code.

Every flag names code inside the scope and tells the truth. I invent nothing. I touch comments and identify refactor targets. I never write application code.

Report only. Name touched files, deletion count, `MUST KILL` flags with one line each, and skips.

# Verify FS-Billing

FS-Billing is a call-billing system. Its user surface is the web app: a customer portal (`/`, `/login`) and an admin shell (`/admin`, `/admin/login`). Both are served by `apps/web` (Vite + React) and call `apps/api` (Fastify + Better Auth) over `/api`. Postgres stores business rows under RLS and the `auth.*` schema. Secondary surfaces:

- **HTTP API.** Driven with `scripts/api.sh`. The contract is `packages/shared/src/api/contract.ts`.
- **Auth CLIs.** `auth:seed`, `auth:unlock`, and `auth:bootstrap` in `apps/api` (`docs/phase-4-api.md`). Run them through `scripts/with-env.sh`.
- **FreeSWITCH/SIPp e2e.** `freeswitch/test/run-integration.sh`. It has no UI and the browser harness does not drive it. See [features/freeswitch-sipp.md](features/freeswitch-sipp.md).
- **Worker** (`apps/worker`). Not started by this skill. The SIPp e2e covers it.

The feature map in [features/README.md](features/README.md) is the source for what to verify and how. Read it before you drive anything.

All commands run from the repo root, with `S=.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts`.

## Isolation rules (read first)

- The user's dev Postgres container `fs-billing-postgres-1` runs on `127.0.0.1:5432`. **Never touch it or its databases.** `sql.sh` and `cleanup.sh` refuse to act on it, and every helper acts only on the container labelled `fs-billing-verify=<RUN_ID>`.
- The root `.env` points at `:5432`, and every pnpm script loads it with `--env-file-if-exists`. Node never lets a `.env` value override a variable that is already exported. `launch.sh` and `with-env.sh` export every DB, auth, and port variable, so repo commands reach the run's container. Never run `pnpm db:*`, `auth:*`, or `test:*` bare in this skill. Always prefix them with `$S/with-env.sh`.
- Do not use `apps/web/e2e` (`pnpm test:e2e`) for verification. It hard-codes ports 3000/4173 and reuses whatever server is already there.
- Every run gets its own container, free ports, secrets, and evidence dir, so two runs can coexist. The SIPp e2e is the exception: it uses fixed host-network ports.

## Launch

```sh
S=.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts
$S/launch.sh            # about 10 s; the last line printed is the RUN_ID
```

From an empty database, `launch.sh` does the following:

1. It picks a `RUN_ID` (`YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ-xxxxxx`) and creates the state dir `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/` (mode 700). It records the run as current in `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/current`.
2. It writes throwaway secrets (owner/app/auth DB passwords, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD`) to `run.env` (mode 600). It never prints them.
3. It starts `docker run postgres:17.9-alpine` as `fsb-verify-<RUN_ID>`, with label `fs-billing-verify=<RUN_ID>`, a free `127.0.0.1` port, and tmpfs data. It waits until TCP `pg_isready` answers, which happens only after image init finishes.
4. It runs migrate, seed, and migrate again to check idempotence (`packages/db/src/bin/*.ts` via `tsx`), then `apps/api/scripts/seed-auth.ts` (`auth:seed`). Every seeded account gets `SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD`.
5. It starts the API (`node --import tsx src/main.ts`) under `setsid` on a free port. `BETTER_AUTH_URL` is set to the API origin, and `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` to the API and web origins.
6. It runs `vite build --outDir <state>/web-dist`, so `apps/web/dist` is never touched. Then it starts `vite preview --strictPort` under `setsid` on a free port, with `FS_BILLING_API_ORIGIN` set so `/api` proxies to this run's API.
7. **Ready** means a real `POST /api/auth/login` as `bjh003@example.com` through the web origin returns 200 with `"kind":"customer"` and `"email":"bjh003@example.com"`, and the logout that follows returns 204. Both transcripts are saved to `docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/launch/ready-login.http`. This is the stack's only login check outside the journeys.

It prints `READY` with the web, API, and Postgres addresses. If it fails, it prints the cleanup command to run for that `RUN_ID`. Prerequisites: Docker, Node 22/24, pnpm 10.34.7, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` already done, Playwright Chromium (`pnpm --filter @fs-billing/web exec playwright install chromium` if missing), `ss`, and `python3`.

To work on an older run, set `VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id>` in front of any helper. Otherwise the helpers use the run in `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/current`.

## Doctor

```sh
$S/doctor.sh            # exit 0 = healthy, 1 = at least one FAIL line
```

Doctor prints one `OK` or `FAIL` line per check:

- The container is running and is ours: id, label, and port all match. If it is not, doctor does not query the database at all.
- The API and web PIDs are alive and carry `VERIFY_RUN_ID=<RUN_ID>` in `/proc/<pid>/environ`. The listener on each port belongs to that process group.
- The git HEAD equals the revision recorded at launch. If HEAD moved, relaunch.
- Applied migrations equal the entries in `packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json`. Tenants and credentials exist. The query goes through `sql.sh`, which repeats the container guards.
- `/api/health` returns `{"status":"ok","service":"api"}`.
- The web serves the built `index.html`, and `/api` proxies to our API.

Doctor is read-only. It sends no login, writes no row, and creates no file, so it is safe to run at any time and never uses up the credential rate limit. Launch's ready check is the login proof. Run doctor first, and again whenever something looks wrong. Do not drive a run that fails doctor.

## Drive

The browser harness is Playwright (`@playwright/test` 1.64 from `apps/web` devDependencies). `drive.mjs` resolves it from there:

```sh
$S/drive.mjs customer-login          # or: admin-login login-errors session-expiry cross-tenant logout theme-and-mobile not-found
$S/drive.mjs customer-login --headed # watch it
$S/drive.mjs all                     # every feature in order, one child process each, then a summary table
```

Before it opens a browser, each journey waits until 61 s have passed since the run's last credential request (see [Gotchas](#gotchas)). It prints `waiting N s for the API's credential rate-limit window (30/min/IP) to reset` when it does. Expect `all` to spend up to a minute per feature on these waits.

Each journey runs headless Chromium (`locale zh-CN`, `Asia/Shanghai`) against the run's web origin. It prints a step table and exits `0` on PASS, `1` on FAIL, or `2` on BLOCKED (precondition missing; the reason is printed). Journeys use only ARIA roles and names taken from `apps/web/src`:

| UI | Handle |
| --- | --- |
| Login form | `getByLabel('登录邮箱')`, `getByLabel('密码', { exact: true })`, `getByRole('button', { name: '登录', exact: true })` |
| Login headings | portal `heading '登录'`, admin `heading '登录管理后台'`; admin page also shows `客户请使用客户门户登录` |
| Login problems | `getByRole('alert')`: `登录邮箱或密码错误` / `请检查后重新输入`; `登录失败次数过多，已暂时锁定` + `请在 M分SS秒 后重试` (also the disabled submit button's name); `该账号已停用` / `如需恢复使用，请联系客服` |
| Expired banner | `getByRole('status')`: `登录已过期，请重新登录` · `登录后将返回之前的页面` (on `?reason=expired`) |
| Expired dialog | `getByRole('dialog', { name: '登录已过期，请重新登录' })` → `button '重新登录'` |
| Portal home | `heading '首页'`, crumb text `ABC001 · 深圳市某某科技有限公司`, `getByRole('region', { name: '当前余额' })` → `-¥35.20` |
| Admin home | `heading '概览'`, text `欢迎，王敏。…`, `html[data-product=admin]` |
| Shell (desktop) | `button '主题'` (◐) → `menuitemradio '浅色' / '深色' / '跟随系统'`; `button '账号菜单'` → `menu` → `menuitem '退出登录'`; `navigation '主导航'` |
| Shell (mobile < 768px) | `button '打开导航'` → `dialog '导航'`; `button '账号菜单'` → `dialog '账号菜单'` with `radiogroup '主题'` and `button '退出登录'` |
| 404 | `heading '找不到这个页面'`, `link '返回首页'`; admin, in the shell: `返回首页即回到「概览」` |

Theme state is `html[data-theme]` plus `localStorage['fs-billing.theme']`. The zone is `html[data-product]`.

HTTP goes through the web origin's `/api` proxy, the same path the browser uses. `api.sh` sends `Origin: <web origin>`, which the API requires on POST:

```sh
$S/api.sh login abc001@example.com a            # seeded password, cookie jar "a"
$S/api.sh login abc001@example.com x --wrong    # fixed wrong password
$S/api.sh GET /api/portal/context a
$S/api.sh POST /api/auth/logout a
$S/api.sh POST /api/admin/portal-accounts/<uuid>/reset-password a
VERIFY_API_BASE=api $S/api.sh GET /api/health  # hit the API origin directly
```

Database access always goes into OUR container through `docker exec`, as the owner role:

```sh
$S/sql.sh "SELECT email_key, failed_count, locked_until FROM auth.login_failures"
$S/sql.sh --value "SELECT count(*) FROM auth.session"   # bare value, for scripts
$S/sql.sh < some.sql
```

Repo CLIs run with the run's environment through `with-env.sh`, which also redacts their output:

```sh
$S/with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:unlock hzx014@example.com   # emergency unlock
$S/with-env.sh pnpm test:idor                                                # IDOR suite, scratch fs_billing_test DB in our container
$S/with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:bootstrap --kind portal --profile <portal-profile-uuid> --replace < /path/to/password-file
```

`auth:bootstrap --generate` prints a new password once, and `with-env.sh` cannot redact a value it does not know. If you use `--generate`, redirect its output into the state dir, not into evidence.

Seeded accounts (from `packages/db/src/seed.ts`; all use `SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD`):

| Account | Role in journeys |
| --- | --- |
| `abc001@example.com` | ABC001, CNY, balance −35.20: main customer |
| `glb009@example.com` | GLB009, USD, −15.02: second tenant (B) |
| `hzx014@example.com` | HZX014: lockout target |
| `cdt018@example.com` | CDT018: tenant `disabled` |
| `wangmin@example.com` | admin 王敏 |
| `lijun@example.com` | admin 李俊: session revocation |
| `bjh003@example.com` | reserved for launch and doctor |

## Evidence

Evidence goes to `docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/<feature-id>/`. Cleanup never deletes it.

- `steps.md` and `result.json`: each step's action, PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED, and the observed detail.
- `sNN-<step>-before` and `sNN-<step>-after` (`.png` full-page screenshot and `.aria.yml` Playwright `ariaSnapshot`): taken automatically around every step, of the page the journey last opened or acted on. A step that drives only HTTP has no page to capture; its proof is the `.http` transcript.
- `NN-name.png` and `NN-name.aria.yml`: named captures of the states a feature file cites, such as `05-locked`.
- `browser-http.txt`: every `/api/*` response the browser saw, with the request body, status, and JSON body. Credential fields are `[REDACTED]`. `NN-name.http` holds the curl transcripts from `api.sh`.
- `NN-name.sql.txt`: the query and its rows from our container. `NN-name.cli.txt`: CLI commands and their output.
- `launch/ready-login.http` and, after cleanup, `logs/` (`*.log.txt`: api, web, build, db-setup, and auth-seed logs, redacted) plus `revision.txt`.

No evidence file ends in `.log`, because the repo's `.gitignore` ignores `*.log`. Every file the helpers write is committable as is: `git add docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>` picks up all of it. Check with `git status --ignored --short docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>`, which must list no `!!` entries.

Every file written into evidence passes through redaction. Secret values from `run.env` become `[REDACTED]`, and so do `session_token=` cookie values and the JSON fields `password`, `currentPassword`, `newPassword`, `temporaryPassword`, `token`, and `secret`. The JSON rule covers values the run never knew, such as the temporary password a real reset returns. Never paste `run.env` or a raw cookie into evidence by hand.

`cleanup.sh` scans the evidence for every `run.env` secret before it deletes the state, and fails, keeping the state, if any file matches. It prints file names only. To scan by hand while the run is live, use `-l` so a hit shows the file and never the secret:

```sh
grep -rlF -e "$(grep ^SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD= /tmp/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/run.env | cut -d= -f2)" docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>   # must print nothing
```

Proof standards:

- **Real user path.** Use the login form, menus, and links in the built app, through the same `/api` proxy. No test-only endpoints, internal setters, or seeded cookies. The API has no test hooks, and the raw Better Auth router is not mounted.
- **Action and resulting state.** Capture the screen before and after, plus the HTTP status and error `code` behind what the UI shows.
- **Side effects in rows.** Check `auth.session` (created on login, deleted on logout and revocation, none kept for disabled accounts), `auth.login_failures` (`failed_count`, `locked_until`, deleted by unlock or a successful login), `auth.account.updated_at` (unchanged by a forbidden reset), `operation_logs`, and `tenant_balances`.
- **DB edits stand in for elapsed time or an operator action.** Setting `auth.session.expires_at` into the past stands in for seven days passing. Deleting session rows is what an admin reset or `--replace` bootstrap does. Each such edit is saved as its own `.sql.txt`, and every journey says so. Never edit DB rows to make an assertion pass.
- **Mocks only at real production boundaries.** This skill mocks nothing. React Query's tab-refocus refetch is triggered with a `visibilitychange` event, the only synthetic browser event in use. It stands in for the user switching tabs, which headless Chromium cannot do.
- **Dry runs.** None of these flows has a dry-run mode. `auth:unlock` really deletes the counter row, and `auth:bootstrap --replace` really revokes sessions. Before you trust any `--dry-run` or "safe" mode added later, prove what it skips with `sql.sh` before and after.
- **Blocked is not passed.** If a precondition is missing (an account locked, seed changed), the journey exits 2 with the reason. Report it as BLOCKED, and never edit the journey to get around it.

## Cleanup

```sh
$S/cleanup.sh                           # current run
VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> $S/cleanup.sh        # a specific run
```

`cleanup.sh` does the following:

- It sends TERM, then KILL after 5 s, to the API and web process groups recorded in `api.pid` and `web.pid`. It does this only while each PID still carries this run's `VERIFY_RUN_ID`. It never kills by name or port. A group that survives KILL is a failure.
- It runs `docker rm -f` on the recorded container id, and only if the id carries label `fs-billing-verify=<RUN_ID>`, then confirms the container is gone. A container that is already gone is fine. A failed removal, or an id with another label, is a failure.
- It copies redacted logs to `<evidence>/logs/` as `*.log.txt`, then runs the leak scan above.
- Only if every step succeeded does it delete `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/` (secrets, cookie jars, web build) and print `evidence kept: <dir> (<n> files)`. Otherwise it prints each failure, keeps the state so you can fix the cause and rerun it, and exits 1.

Run cleanup after every run, including failed launches. Confirm with `ls docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/` and `docker ps --filter label=fs-billing-verify`.

Commit evidence only when a PR needs the proof. Every file is committable (see [Evidence](#evidence)).

## Helpers

| Script | Invocation | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `launch.sh` | `$S/launch.sh` | Isolated stack from an empty DB; prints RUN_ID |
| `doctor.sh` | `$S/doctor.sh` | Read-only health and ownership check (no login, no writes) |
| `drive.mjs` | `$S/drive.mjs <feature\|all> [--headed]` | Playwright journey and evidence for one feature, or all in turn |
| `api.sh` | `$S/api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]` / `$S/api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json]` | Redacted HTTP transcript via the web proxy |
| `sql.sh` | `$S/sql.sh [--value] "<sql>"` / `$S/sql.sh < file.sql` | psql as owner in OUR container only |
| `with-env.sh` | `$S/with-env.sh <command...>` | Repo CLI with the run's env, output redacted |
| `cleanup.sh` | `$S/cleanup.sh` | Kill our process groups, remove our container, keep evidence; exit 1 and keep state on any failure |
| `lib.sh` | sourced | Run resolution, redaction, leak scan, ownership checks |

Typical loop:

```sh
S=.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts
RUN_ID=$($S/launch.sh | tail -n1)
$S/doctor.sh
$S/drive.mjs customer-login    # or: $S/drive.mjs all
$S/cleanup.sh
ls docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/$RUN_ID/customer-login
```

## Gotchas

- **Credential rate limit.** `POST /api/auth/login` and `/api/auth/change-password` allow 30 requests per minute per client IP, in a fixed window that opens at the first request. Every browser and `api.sh` request in a run comes from `127.0.0.1`, so they all share one budget. Running features back to back used to hit it: `not-found` got `429 RATE_LIMITED` right after `login-errors`. Each journey now waits until 61 s have passed since the run's last credential request, which `api.sh` and `drive.mjs` record in the state dir. If you send logins by hand between journeys, they count too, and the next journey waits for them. Never raise or disable the API's limit to make a run pass. A `429 RATE_LIMITED` in a journey means the wait was bypassed; wait a minute and rerun that feature.
- **Doctor is read-only on purpose.** Do not add a login to it. A login uses up the rate-limit budget and writes a session row. Launch's ready check already proves login and logout.

Keep the map honest as the app grows: run `/maintain-verification-skill`.
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/api.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# HTTP against this run's stack, through the web origin's /api proxy (the browser's path).
     3	# Usage:
     4	#   api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]     POST /api/auth/login with the seeded password
     5	#                                            (or a fixed wrong one); cookies go to jar <jar>
     6	#   api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body] any other request, optionally with jar cookies
     7	# Prints a redacted HTTP transcript (request line, response status, headers, body).
     8	# Set VERIFY_API_BASE=api to hit the API origin directly instead of the web proxy.
     9	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
    10	verify_load
    11	
    12	base=$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN
    13	[[ ${VERIFY_API_BASE:-web} == api ]] && base=$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN
    14	mkdir -p "$STATE/jars"
    15	
    16	body_file=
    17	cleanup_body() { [[ -n $body_file ]] && rm -f "$body_file"; return 0; }
    18	trap cleanup_body EXIT
    19	
    20	if [[ ${1:-} == login ]]; then
    21	  [[ $# -ge 3 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]'
    22	  email=$2 jar=$3 method=POST path=/api/auth/login
    23	  body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
    24	  VERIFY_EMAIL=$email VERIFY_WRONG=${4:-} python3 -c '
    25	import json, os
    26	pw = "wrong-password-verify" if os.environ["VERIFY_WRONG"] == "--wrong" else os.environ["SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD"]
    27	print(json.dumps({"email": os.environ["VERIFY_EMAIL"], "password": pw}), end="")' > "$body_file"
    28	  shown_body="{\"email\":\"$email\",\"password\":\"[REDACTED]\"}"
    29	else
    30	  [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body]'
    31	  method=$1 path=$2 jar=${3:-} shown_body=${4:-}
    32	  if [[ -n ${4:-} ]]; then
    33	    body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
    34	    printf '%s' "$4" > "$body_file"
    35	  fi
    36	fi
    37	
    38	case $path in /api/auth/login|/api/auth/change-password) verify_note_credential_request ;; esac
    39	
    40	args=(-sS -i -X "$method" -H "Origin: $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" --max-time 20)
    41	[[ -n $jar ]] && args+=(-b "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt" -c "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt")
    42	[[ -n $body_file ]] && args+=(-H 'Content-Type: application/json' --data-binary "@$body_file")
    43	
    44	{
    45	  printf '> %s %s%s\n' "$method" "$base" "$path"
    46	  printf '> Origin: %s\n' "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN"
    47	  [[ -n $jar ]] && printf '> Cookie: (jar %s)\n' "$jar"
    48	  [[ -n $shown_body ]] && printf '> Body: %s\n' "$shown_body"
    49	  printf '\n'
    50	  curl "${args[@]}" "$base$path"
    51	  printf '\n'
    52	} | tr -d '\r' | verify_redact
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/cleanup.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Tear down one run: kill only the process groups it recorded, remove only its labelled
     3	# container, copy redacted logs into evidence, delete scratch state. Evidence is kept.
     4	# State (and its secrets) is deleted only when every step succeeded, so a failed cleanup
     5	# can be rerun. Exit 1 on any failure.
     6	# Usage: cleanup.sh            (VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> cleanup.sh for a specific run)
     7	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
     8	verify_load
     9	
    10	failures=0
    11	fail() { echo "cleanup: $*" >&2; failures=$((failures + 1)); }
    12	
    13	for name in web api; do
    14	  pid=$(cat "$STATE/$name.pid" 2>/dev/null || true)
    15	  [[ -n $pid ]] || continue
    16	  if verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then
    17	    kill -TERM -- "-$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
    18	    for _ in $(seq 1 50); do kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null || break; sleep 0.1; done
    19	    kill -KILL -- "-$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
    20	    sleep 0.2
    21	    if verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then fail "$name process group $pid survived KILL"; else echo "stopped $name (process group $pid)"; fi
    22	  else
    23	    echo "$name pid $pid is gone or not from run $RUN_ID; not killing it"
    24	  fi
    25	done
    26	
    27	id=$(cat "$STATE/container.id" 2>/dev/null || true)
    28	if [[ -z $id ]]; then
    29	  echo 'no container id recorded; nothing removed'
    30	elif [[ $VERIFY_CONTAINER == "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]]; then
    31	  fail "refusing to touch $VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER"
    32	elif ! label=$(docker inspect -f '{{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}}' "$id" 2>/dev/null); then
    33	  echo "container ${id:0:12} is already gone"
    34	elif [[ $label != "$RUN_ID" ]]; then
    35	  fail "container ${id:0:12} is labelled '$label', not $RUN_ID; not removing it"
    36	elif docker rm -f "$id" >/dev/null && ! docker inspect "$id" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    37	  echo "removed container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12})"
    38	else
    39	  fail "could not remove container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12})"
    40	fi
    41	
    42	mkdir -p "$EVIDENCE/logs" || fail "cannot create $EVIDENCE/logs"
    43	for log in "$STATE"/*.log; do
    44	  [[ -f $log ]] || continue
    45	  verify_redact < "$log" > "$EVIDENCE/logs/$(basename "$log" .log).log.txt" || fail "could not copy $log"
    46	done
    47	for file in revision.txt worktree.txt; do
    48	  [[ -f $STATE/$file ]] && { cp "$STATE/$file" "$EVIDENCE/logs/" || fail "could not copy $file"; }
    49	done
    50	
    51	if ! leaks=$(verify_leak_scan "$EVIDENCE"); then
    52	  fail "run secrets found in evidence files (names only): ${leaks//$'\n'/ }"
    53	fi
    54	
    55	if (( failures )); then
    56	  echo "cleanup: $failures failure(s); state kept at $STATE so you can fix and rerun" >&2
    57	  exit 1
    58	fi
    59	
    60	case $STATE in
    61	  "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT"/*) rm -rf -- "$STATE" ;;
    62	esac
    63	[[ $(cat "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current" 2>/dev/null) == "$RUN_ID" ]] && rm -f "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current"
    64	echo "evidence kept: $EVIDENCE ($(find "$EVIDENCE" -type f | wc -l | tr -d ' ') files)"
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/doctor.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Is this run's instance worth driving? Prints one OK/FAIL line per check; exit 1 on any FAIL.
     3	# Usage: doctor.sh            (VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> doctor.sh for a specific run)
     4	# Read-only: no logins, no DB writes, no files. Launch's ready check is the login proof.
     5	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
     6	verify_load
     7	set +e
     8	
     9	failures=0
    10	ok() { printf 'OK    %s\n' "$*"; }
    11	bad() { printf 'FAIL  %s\n' "$*"; failures=$((failures + 1)); }
    12	echo "run $RUN_ID"
    13	
    14	# Container: ours by name, id, label, and port; never the user's.
    15	id=$(cat "$STATE/container.id" 2>/dev/null)
    16	read -r running label port < <(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}} {{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}} {{(index (index .NetworkSettings.Ports "5432/tcp") 0).HostPort}}' "$id" 2>/dev/null)
    17	container_ok=false
    18	if [[ $running == true && $label == "$RUN_ID" && $port == "$VERIFY_PG_PORT" && $VERIFY_CONTAINER != "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]]; then
    19	  container_ok=true
    20	  ok "postgres container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12}) running, labelled, 127.0.0.1:$port"
    21	else
    22	  bad "postgres container $VERIFY_CONTAINER running=$running label=$label port=$port"
    23	fi
    24	
    25	# Processes: alive, carry our VERIFY_RUN_ID, and own their listening port.
    26	check_proc() {
    27	  local name=$1 pidfile=$2 port=$3 pid listeners owned=false l
    28	  pid=$(cat "$pidfile" 2>/dev/null)
    29	  if [[ -z $pid ]] || ! verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then bad "$name pid ${pid:-?} not alive or not from run $RUN_ID"; return; fi
    30	  listeners=$(verify_port_pids "$port")
    31	  for l in $listeners; do
    32	    [[ $(ps -o pgid= -p "$l" | tr -d ' ') == "$pid" ]] && verify_owns_pid "$l" && owned=true
    33	  done
    34	  if $owned; then ok "$name pid $pid alive, owns 127.0.0.1:$port"; else bad "$name port $port listeners [${listeners//$'\n'/ }] not in pgid $pid"; fi
    35	}
    36	check_proc api "$STATE/api.pid" "$VERIFY_API_PORT"
    37	check_proc web "$STATE/web.pid" "$VERIFY_WEB_PORT"
    38	
    39	# Revision: what was launched vs what is checked out now.
    40	launched=$(cat "$STATE/revision.txt") head=$(git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" rev-parse HEAD)
    41	dirty=$(git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" status --short | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
    42	if [[ $launched == "$head" ]]; then ok "git $head ($dirty uncommitted paths)"; else bad "launched at $launched but HEAD is now $head; relaunch"; fi
    43	
    44	# Database: reachable, every journal migration applied, seed and auth seed present.
    45	# Only through sql.sh's guards, and only when the container check above passed.
    46	expected=$(python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(len(json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))["entries"]))' "$VERIFY_REPO/packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json")
    47	if ! $container_ok; then
    48	  bad 'db: not queried, the container is not ours'
    49	else
    50	  row=$("$(dirname "$0")/sql.sh" --value \
    51	    "SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM drizzle.__drizzle_migrations), (SELECT count(*) FROM tenants), (SELECT count(*) FROM auth.account WHERE provider_id='credential')" 2>&1)
    52	  IFS='|' read -r applied tenants creds <<< "$row"
    53	  if [[ $applied == "$expected" && $tenants -gt 0 && $creds -gt 0 ]]; then
    54	    ok "db migrations $applied/$expected, tenants $tenants, credentials $creds"
    55	  else
    56	    bad "db: migrations ${applied:-?}/$expected tenants ${tenants:-?} credentials ${creds:-?} ($row)"
    57	  fi
    58	fi
    59	
    60	# API and web.
    61	health=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN/api/health")
    62	[[ $health == '{"status":"ok","service":"api"}' ]] && ok "api health $health" || bad "api health: ${health:-no answer}"
    63	html=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN/login")
    64	[[ $html == *'<div id="root">'* && $html == *'/assets/'* ]] && ok "web serves built app at $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" || bad 'web did not serve the built index.html'
    65	proxied=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN/api/health")
    66	[[ $proxied == "$health" && -n $health ]] && ok 'web proxies /api to our API' || bad "web /api proxy: ${proxied:-no answer}"
    67	
    68	if (( failures )); then echo "doctor: $failures check(s) failed"; exit 1; fi
    69	echo 'doctor: healthy'
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/launch.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Start an isolated FS-Billing stack: dedicated Postgres container, API, web preview.
     3	# Usage: launch.sh            (prints RUN_ID on the last line)
     4	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
     5	
     6	command -v docker >/dev/null || die 'docker is required'
     7	command -v ss >/dev/null || die 'ss (iproute2) is required for port ownership checks'
     8	[[ -d $VERIFY_REPO/node_modules/.pnpm ]] || die 'run pnpm install --frozen-lockfile first'
     9	
    10	RUN_ID=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)-$(python3 -c 'import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(3))')
    11	STATE=$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/$RUN_ID
    12	EVIDENCE=$VERIFY_EVIDENCE_ROOT/$RUN_ID
    13	umask 077
    14	mkdir -p "$STATE" "$EVIDENCE/launch"
    15	chmod 700 "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT" "$STATE"
    16	printf '%s\n' "$RUN_ID" > "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current"
    17	trap 'status=$?; (( status == 0 )) || echo "launch failed; logs in $STATE; tear down with: VERIFY_RUN_ID=$RUN_ID $(dirname "$0")/cleanup.sh" >&2' EXIT
    18	
    19	container=fsb-verify-$RUN_ID
    20	pg_port=$(verify_free_port)
    21	api_port=$(verify_free_port)
    22	web_port=$(verify_free_port)
    23	while [[ $api_port == "$pg_port" ]]; do api_port=$(verify_free_port); done
    24	while [[ $web_port == "$pg_port" || $web_port == "$api_port" ]]; do web_port=$(verify_free_port); done
    25	secret() { python3 -c 'import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(24))'; }
    26	api_origin=http://127.0.0.1:$api_port
    27	web_origin=http://127.0.0.1:$web_port
    28	
    29	# Every database/auth/port variable the root .env defines is set here, so even a
    30	# command that loads .env cannot fall through to the user's database on :5432.
    31	pg_owner=$(secret) pg_app=$(secret) pg_auth=$(secret)
    32	cat > "$STATE/run.env" <<EOF
    33	VERIFY_RUN_ID=$RUN_ID
    34	VERIFY_CONTAINER=$container
    35	VERIFY_PG_PORT=$pg_port
    36	VERIFY_API_PORT=$api_port
    37	VERIFY_WEB_PORT=$web_port
    38	VERIFY_API_ORIGIN=$api_origin
    39	VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN=$web_origin
    40	PG_OWNER_PASSWORD=$pg_owner
    41	PG_APP_PASSWORD=$pg_app
    42	PG_AUTH_PASSWORD=$pg_auth
    43	POSTGRES_DB=fs_billing
    44	POSTGRES_USER=fs_billing
    45	POSTGRES_PASSWORD=$pg_owner
    46	POSTGRES_PORT=$pg_port
    47	DATABASE_URL=postgresql://fs_billing:$pg_owner@127.0.0.1:$pg_port/fs_billing
    48	APP_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://fs_billing_app:$pg_app@127.0.0.1:$pg_port/fs_billing
    49	AUTH_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://fs_billing_auth:$pg_auth@127.0.0.1:$pg_port/fs_billing
    50	API_HOST=127.0.0.1
    51	API_PORT=$api_port
    52	BETTER_AUTH_SECRET=$(secret)
    53	BETTER_AUTH_URL=$api_origin
    54	BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS=$api_origin,$web_origin
    55	SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD=$(secret)
    56	FS_BILLING_API_ORIGIN=$api_origin
    57	NODE_ENV=development
    58	EOF
    59	chmod 600 "$STATE/run.env"
    60	set -a; source "$STATE/run.env"; set +a
    61	export RUN_ID STATE EVIDENCE
    62	git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" rev-parse HEAD > "$STATE/revision.txt"
    63	git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" status --short > "$STATE/worktree.txt"
    64	
    65	echo "[1/6] Postgres container $container on 127.0.0.1:$pg_port"
    66	docker run -d --name "$container" --label "fs-billing-verify=$RUN_ID" \
    67	  -e POSTGRES_DB -e POSTGRES_USER -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD -e TZ=UTC -e PGTZ=UTC \
    68	  -p "127.0.0.1:$pg_port:5432" --tmpfs /var/lib/postgresql/data \
    69	  postgres:17.9-alpine > "$STATE/container.id"
    70	# The image's init server listens on the socket only; TCP answers once init is done.
    71	for _ in $(seq 1 120); do
    72	  docker exec "$container" pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U fs_billing -d fs_billing >/dev/null 2>&1 && break
    73	  sleep 0.5
    74	done
    75	docker exec "$container" pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U fs_billing -d fs_billing >/dev/null
    76	
    77	# tsx is called directly (not the pnpm scripts) so the root .env is never loaded.
    78	echo '[2/6] migrate + seed + migrate (idempotence) + auth:seed'
    79	(
    80	  cd "$VERIFY_REPO/packages/db"
    81	  pnpm exec tsx src/bin/migrate.ts
    82	  pnpm exec tsx src/bin/seed.ts
    83	  pnpm exec tsx src/bin/migrate.ts
    84	) > "$STATE/db-setup.log" 2>&1
    85	(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/api" && node --import tsx scripts/seed-auth.ts) > "$STATE/auth-seed.log" 2>&1
    86	
    87	echo "[3/6] API on $api_origin"
    88	(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/api" && exec setsid node --import tsx src/main.ts) > "$STATE/api.log" 2>&1 &
    89	echo $! > "$STATE/api.pid"
    90	for _ in $(seq 1 120); do
    91	  curl -sf "$api_origin/api/health" >/dev/null 2>&1 && break
    92	  kill -0 "$(cat "$STATE/api.pid")" 2>/dev/null || die "API exited; see $STATE/api.log"
    93	  sleep 0.5
    94	done
    95	curl -sf "$api_origin/api/health" >/dev/null
    96	
    97	echo "[4/6] web build into $STATE/web-dist"
    98	(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/web" && pnpm exec vite build --outDir "$STATE/web-dist" --emptyOutDir) > "$STATE/web-build.log" 2>&1
    99	
   100	echo "[5/6] web preview on $web_origin (proxies /api to $api_origin)"
   101	(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/web" && exec setsid node_modules/.bin/vite preview --host 127.0.0.1 --port "$web_port" --strictPort --outDir "$STATE/web-dist") > "$STATE/web.log" 2>&1 &
   102	echo $! > "$STATE/web.pid"
   103	for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
   104	  curl -sf "$web_origin/" >/dev/null 2>&1 && break
   105	  kill -0 "$(cat "$STATE/web.pid")" 2>/dev/null || die "web exited; see $STATE/web.log"
   106	  sleep 0.5
   107	done
   108	
   109	echo '[6/6] ready check: real seeded login and logout through the web origin'
   110	ready=$EVIDENCE/launch/ready-login.http
   111	"$(dirname "$0")/api.sh" login bjh003@example.com ready > "$ready"
   112	grep -q '^HTTP/1.1 200' "$ready" && grep -q '"kind":"customer"' "$ready" && grep -q '"email":"bjh003@example.com"' "$ready" \
   113	  || die "ready login is not 200 customer bjh003@example.com; see $ready"
   114	"$(dirname "$0")/api.sh" POST /api/auth/logout ready > "$STATE/ready-logout.http"
   115	cat "$STATE/ready-logout.http" >> "$ready"
   116	grep -q '^HTTP/1.1 204' "$STATE/ready-logout.http" || die "ready logout is not 204; see $ready"
   117	rm -f "$STATE/ready-logout.http" "$STATE/jars/ready.txt"
   118	
   119	cat <<EOF
   120	READY
   121	  run id    $RUN_ID
   122	  web       $web_origin
   123	  api       $api_origin
   124	  postgres  127.0.0.1:$pg_port (container $container)
   125	  state     $STATE (secrets, pids; removed by cleanup.sh)
   126	  evidence  $EVIDENCE (kept)
   127	$RUN_ID
   128	EOF
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/lib.sh
     1	# Sourced by the verify-fs-billing helpers. Not executable on its own.
     2	# shellcheck shell=bash
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	VERIFY_SKILL_DIR=$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)
     6	VERIFY_REPO=$(git -C "$VERIFY_SKILL_DIR" rev-parse --show-toplevel)
     7	VERIFY_STATE_ROOT=${VERIFY_STATE_ROOT:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/verify-fs-billing}
     8	VERIFY_EVIDENCE_ROOT=$VERIFY_REPO/docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing
     9	# The user's own dev database. Never touched by this skill.
    10	VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER=fs-billing-postgres-1
    11	
    12	die() { printf 'verify-fs-billing: %s\n' "$*" >&2; exit 1; }
    13	
    14	# Resolve the run: $VERIFY_RUN_ID, else the run launch.sh recorded last.
    15	verify_run_id() {
    16	  if [[ -n ${VERIFY_RUN_ID:-} ]]; then printf '%s\n' "$VERIFY_RUN_ID"; return; fi
    17	  [[ -f $VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current ]] || die "no run recorded; run launch.sh or set VERIFY_RUN_ID"
    18	  cat "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current"
    19	}
    20	
    21	# Load a run's state into the environment: RUN_ID, STATE, EVIDENCE, and run.env.
    22	verify_load() {
    23	  RUN_ID=$(verify_run_id)
    24	  [[ $RUN_ID =~ ^[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{6}Z-[0-9a-f]{6}$ ]] || die "malformed run id: $RUN_ID"
    25	  STATE=$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/$RUN_ID
    26	  EVIDENCE=$VERIFY_EVIDENCE_ROOT/$RUN_ID
    27	  [[ -f $STATE/run.env ]] || die "no state for run $RUN_ID at $STATE"
    28	  set -a
    29	  # shellcheck disable=SC1091
    30	  source "$STATE/run.env"
    31	  set +a
    32	  export RUN_ID STATE EVIDENCE
    33	}
    34	
    35	VERIFY_SECRET_KEYS=(BETTER_AUTH_SECRET SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD PG_OWNER_PASSWORD PG_APP_PASSWORD PG_AUTH_PASSWORD)
    36	# JSON fields that carry a credential, including ones the run never knew (a reset's
    37	# temporaryPassword). Keep in sync with sensitiveJsonFields in drive.mjs.
    38	VERIFY_SENSITIVE_JSON='password|currentPassword|newPassword|temporaryPassword|token|secret'
    39	
    40	# Replace every secret value from run.env with [REDACTED], plus cookie values and
    41	# sensitive JSON fields.
    42	verify_redact() {
    43	  local args=() key value
    44	  for key in "${VERIFY_SECRET_KEYS[@]}"; do
    45	    value=${!key:-}
    46	    [[ -n $value ]] && args+=(-e "s|${value}|[REDACTED]|g")
    47	  done
    48	  args+=(-e 's/(session_token=)[^;[:space:]"]+/\1[REDACTED]/g')
    49	  args+=(-e "s/(\"(${VERIFY_SENSITIVE_JSON})\"[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*\")([^\"\\\\]|\\\\.)*\"/\\1[REDACTED]\"/g")
    50	  sed -E "${args[@]}"
    51	}
    52	
    53	# Evidence files that contain any run.env secret. Prints file names only, never the match.
    54	verify_leak_scan() {
    55	  local key value found=0
    56	  for key in "${VERIFY_SECRET_KEYS[@]}"; do
    57	    value=${!key:-}
    58	    [[ -n $value ]] || continue
    59	    grep -rlF -e "$value" -- "$1" && found=1
    60	  done
    61	  return $(( found ))
    62	}
    63	
    64	# The API allows 30 credential requests (login, change-password) per minute per IP in a
    65	# fixed window. Record each one so the next journey can wait out the window.
    66	verify_note_credential_request() {
    67	  date +%s > "$STATE/credential-at"
    68	}
    69	
    70	verify_free_port() {
    71	  python3 -c 'import socket; s=socket.socket(); s.bind(("127.0.0.1",0)); print(s.getsockname()[1]); s.close()'
    72	}
    73	
    74	# True when pid is alive and carries this run's VERIFY_RUN_ID in its environment.
    75	verify_owns_pid() {
    76	  local pid=$1
    77	  [[ -r /proc/$pid/environ ]] || return 1
    78	  tr '\0' '\n' < "/proc/$pid/environ" | grep -qx "VERIFY_RUN_ID=$RUN_ID"
    79	}
    80	
    81	# PIDs listening on 127.0.0.1:<port>.
    82	verify_port_pids() {
    83	  ss -Hltnp "sport = :$1" 2>/dev/null | grep -o 'pid=[0-9]*' | cut -d= -f2 | sort -u
    84	}
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/sql.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Run SQL as the owner role inside THIS run's Postgres container (never the user's).
     3	# Usage: sql.sh "SELECT ..."      or      sql.sh < file.sql
     4	#        sql.sh --value "SELECT ..."   unaligned, tuples only (one value per line)
     5	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
     6	verify_load
     7	
     8	[[ $VERIFY_CONTAINER != "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]] || die 'refusing to touch the user container'
     9	[[ $(docker inspect -f '{{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}}' "$VERIFY_CONTAINER" 2>/dev/null) == "$RUN_ID" ]] \
    10	  || die "container $VERIFY_CONTAINER is not labelled for run $RUN_ID"
    11	
    12	psql=(docker exec -i "$VERIFY_CONTAINER" psql -U fs_billing -d fs_billing -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -P pager=off)
    13	if [[ ${1:-} == --value ]]; then psql+=(-Atq); shift; fi
    14	if [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; then
    15	  "${psql[@]}" -c "$*"
    16	else
    17	  "${psql[@]}"
    18	fi
.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/with-env.sh
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Run a repo command with this run's environment (our DB URLs, auth secret, seed password).
     3	# Exported values win over the root .env, which every pnpm script loads with
     4	# --env-file-if-exists, so documented commands hit our container, not :5432.
     5	# Usage: with-env.sh <command...>     e.g. with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:unlock hzx014@example.com
     6	# Output is redacted; secrets never reach the terminal.
     7	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
     8	verify_load
     9	[[ $# -gt 0 ]] || die 'usage: with-env.sh <command...>'
    10	cd "$VERIFY_REPO"
    11	set +e
    12	"$@" 2>&1 | verify_redact | sed -E 's#(postgres(ql)?://[^:/@]+:)[^@]+@#\1[REDACTED]@#g'
    13	exit "${PIPESTATUS[0]}"

     2	// Drive one mapped feature of a launched run through the real browser path.

    47	// The API allows 30 credential requests per minute per IP in a fixed window that opens at

    51	class Precondition extends Error {}
    52	const steps = [];
    53	function write(name, content) {
    54	  writeFileSync(join(out, name), redact(content));
    55	}
    56	function sql(name, query) {
    57	  const result = execFileSync(join(scripts, 'sql.sh'), [query], { encoding: 'utf8', env: { ...process.env, VERIFY_RUN_ID: runId } });
    58	  write(`${name}.sql.txt`, `-- ${query}\n${result}`);

    61	/** Single value from the run's DB, through sql.sh's container guards. */
    62	function scalar(query) {

    64	}
    65	function api(name, args) {
    66	  const result = execFileSync(join(scripts, 'api.sh'), args, { encoding: 'utf8', env: { ...process.env, VERIFY_RUN_ID: runId } });
    67	  write(`${name}.http`, result);
    68	  const status = Number(/^HTTP\/1\.1 (\d+)/m.exec(result)?.[1]);
    69	  const bodyText = result.trim().split('\n').at(-1);
    70	  let body = null;
    71	  try {
    72	    body = JSON.parse(bodyText);
    73	  } catch {}
    74	  return { status, body };
    75	}
    76	/** Repo command through with-env.sh (run env wins over the root .env); output is redacted. */
    77	function cli(name, args) {
    78	  let result;
    79	  try {
    80	    result = execFileSync(join(scripts, 'with-env.sh'), args, { encoding: 'utf8', env: { ...process.env, VERIFY_RUN_ID: runId } });
    81	  } catch (error) {
    82	    write(`${name}.cli.txt`, `$ ${args.join(' ')}\n${error.stdout ?? ''}`);
    83	    throw new Error(`${args.join(' ')} exited ${error.status}`);
    84	  }
    85	  write(`${name}.cli.txt`, `$ ${args.join(' ')}\n${result}`);

    87	}
    88	/** The page the journey last opened or acted on; steps capture it before and after. */
    89	let focus;
    90	async function capture(name) {
    91	  if (!focus || focus.isClosed() || focus.url() === 'about:blank') return;
    92	  try {
    93	    await aria(focus, name);
    94	    await shot(focus, name);
    95	  } catch {}
    96	}
    97	async function step(id, action, fn) {
    98	  const started = Date.now();
    99	  const prefix = `s${String(steps.length + 1).padStart(2, '0')}-${id}`;
   100	  await capture(`${prefix}-before`);
   101	  try {
   102	    const detail = await fn();
   103	    steps.push({ id, action, result: 'PASS', detail: detail ?? '', ms: Date.now() - started });
   104	  } catch (error) {
   105	    steps.push({ id, action, result: error instanceof Precondition ? 'BLOCKED' : 'FAIL', detail: String(error?.message ?? error).split('\n').slice(0, 6).join(' / '), ms: Date.now() - started });
   106	    throw error;
   107	  } finally {
   108	    await capture(`${prefix}-after`);
   109	  }
   110	}

   112	let browser;
   113	async function newPage(options = {}) {
   114	  const context = await browser.newContext({ baseURL: web, locale: 'zh-CN', timezoneId: 'Asia/Shanghai', ...options });
   115	  const page = await context.newPage();
   116	  focus = page;
   117	  page.on('request', (request) => {
   118	    if (credentialPaths.has(new URL(request.url()).pathname)) writeFileSync(credentialStamp, String(Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000)));
   119	  });
   120	  page.on('response', async (response) => {
   121	    const url = new URL(response.url());
   122	    if (!url.pathname.startsWith('/api/')) return;
   123	    let body = '';
   124	    try {
   125	      body = await response.text();
   126	    } catch {}
   127	    const sent = response.request().postData();
   128	    const shown = sent ?? '';
   129	    appendFileSync(join(out, httpLog), redact(`${new Date().toISOString()} ${response.request().method()} ${url.pathname}${url.search} ${shown} -> ${response.status()} ${body}\n`));
   130	  });
   131	  return page;
   132	}
   133	async function shot(page, name) {
   134	  await page.screenshot({ path: join(out, `${name}.png`), fullPage: true });
   135	}
   136	async function aria(page, name, locator = page.locator('body')) {
   137	  write(`${name}.aria.yml`, await locator.ariaSnapshot());
   138	}
   139	function path(page) {
   140	  const url = new URL(page.url());
   141	  return `${url.pathname}${url.search}`;
   142	}
   143	async function expectPath(page, expected) {
   144	  focus = page;
   145	  await expect.poll(() => path(page), { timeout: 10_000 }).toBe(expected);
   146	}
   147	/** Fill and submit the login form; resolves with the /api/auth/login response. */
   148	async function submitLogin(page, email, secret = password) {
   149	  focus = page;
   150	  await page.getByLabel('登录邮箱').fill(email);
   151	  await page.getByLabel('密码', { exact: true }).fill(secret);
   152	  const response = page.waitForResponse((r) => new URL(r.url()).pathname === '/api/auth/login' && r.request().method() === 'POST');
   153	  await page.getByRole('button', { name: '登录', exact: true }).click();
   154	  return response;
   155	}
   156	async function loginAs(page, loginPath, email) {
   157	  await page.goto(loginPath);
   158	  const response = await submitLogin(page, email);
   159	  if (response.status() !== 200) throw new Error(`login ${email} -> ${response.status()} ${await response.text()}`);
   160	}
   161	const sessionsOf = (email) => `SELECT count(*) FROM auth.session s JOIN auth."user" u ON u.id = s.user_id WHERE u.email = '${email}' AND s.expires_at > now()`;
   162	function requireUnlocked(email) {
   163	  const row = scalar(`SELECT coalesce(max(locked_until)::text, '') FROM auth.login_failures WHERE email_key = '${email}' AND locked_until > now()`);
   164	  if (row) throw new Precondition(`${email} is locked until ${row}; run auth:unlock (see login-errors.md) or relaunch`);
   165	}

   167	const journeys = {
   168	  async 'customer-login'() {
   169	    requireUnlocked('abc001@example.com');
   170	    const page = await newPage();
   171	    await step('entry-redirect', 'visit / signed out', async () => {
   172	      await page.goto('/');

   179	    await step('portal-login', 'submit abc001@example.com + seeded password on /login', async () => {
   180	      const response = await submitLogin(page, 'abc001@example.com');
   181	      expect(response.status()).toBe(200);

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# HTTP against this run's stack, through the web origin's /api proxy (the browser's path).
     3	# Usage:
     4	#   api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]     POST /api/auth/login with the seeded password
     5	#                                            (or a fixed wrong one); cookies go to jar <jar>
     6	#   api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body] any other request, optionally with jar cookies
     7	# Prints a redacted HTTP transcript (request line, response status, headers, body).
     8	# Set VERIFY_API_BASE=api to hit the API origin directly instead of the web proxy.
     9	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
    10	verify_load
    11	
    12	base=$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN
    13	[[ ${VERIFY_API_BASE:-web} == api ]] && base=$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN
    14	mkdir -p "$STATE/jars"
    15	
    16	body_file=
    17	cleanup_body() { [[ -n $body_file ]] && rm -f "$body_file"; return 0; }
    18	trap cleanup_body EXIT
    19	
    20	if [[ ${1:-} == login ]]; then
    21	  [[ $# -ge 3 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]'
    22	  email=$2 jar=$3 method=POST path=/api/auth/login
    23	  body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
    24	  VERIFY_EMAIL=$email VERIFY_WRONG=${4:-} python3 -c '
    25	import json, os
    26	pw = "wrong-password-verify" if os.environ["VERIFY_WRONG"] == "--wrong" else os.environ["SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD"]
    27	print(json.dumps({"email": os.environ["VERIFY_EMAIL"], "password": pw}), end="")' > "$body_file"
    28	  shown_body="{\"email\":\"$email\",\"password\":\"[REDACTED]\"}"
    29	else
    30	  [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body]'
    31	  method=$1 path=$2 jar=${3:-} shown_body=${4:-}
    32	  if [[ -n ${4:-} ]]; then
    33	    body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
    34	    printf '%s' "$4" > "$body_file"
    35	  fi
    36	fi
    37	
    38	case $path in /api/auth/login|/api/auth/change-password) verify_note_credential_request ;; esac
    39	
    40	args=(-sS -i -X "$method" -H "Origin: $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" --max-time 20)
    41	[[ -n $jar ]] && args+=(-b "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt" -c "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt")
    42	[[ -n $body_file ]] && args+=(-H 'Content-Type: application/json' --data-binary "@$body_file")
    43	
    44	{
    45	  printf '> %s %s%s\n' "$method" "$base" "$path"
    46	  printf '> Origin: %s\n' "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN"
    47	  [[ -n $jar ]] && printf '> Cookie: (jar %s)\n' "$jar"
    48	  [[ -n $shown_body ]] && printf '> Body: %s\n' "$shown_body"
    49	  printf '\n'
    50	  curl "${args[@]}" "$base$path"
    51	  printf '\n'
    52	} | tr -d '\r' | verify_redact
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Tear down one run: kill only the process groups it recorded, remove only its labelled
     3	# container, copy redacted logs into evidence, delete scratch state. Evidence is kept.
     4	# State (and its secrets) is deleted only when every step succeeded, so a failed cleanup
     5	# can be rerun. Exit 1 on any failure.
     6	# Usage: cleanup.sh            (VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> cleanup.sh for a specific run)
     7	source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
     8	verify_load
     9	
    10	failures=0
    11	fail() { echo "cleanup: $*" >&2; failures=$((failures + 1)); }
    12	
    13	for name in web api; do
    14	  pid=$(cat "$STATE/$name.pid" 2>/dev/null || true)
    15	  [[ -n $pid ]] || continue
    16	  if verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then
    17	    kill -TERM -- "-$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
    18	    for _ in $(seq 1 50); do kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null || break; sleep 0.1; done
    19	    kill -KILL -- "-$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
    20	    sleep 0.2
    21	    if verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then fail "$name process group $pid survived KILL"; else echo "stopped $name (process group $pid)"; fi
    22	  else
    23	    echo "$name pid $pid is gone or not from run $RUN_ID; not killing it"
    24	  fi
    25	done
    26	
    27	id=$(cat "$STATE/container.id" 2>/dev/null || true)
    28	if [[ -z $id ]]; then
    29	  echo 'no container id recorded; nothing removed'
    30	elif [[ $VERIFY_CONTAINER == "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]]; then
    31	  fail "refusing to touch $VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER"
    32	elif ! label=$(docker inspect -f '{{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}}' "$id" 2>/dev/null); then
    33	  echo "container ${id:0:12} is already gone"
    34	elif [[ $label != "$RUN_ID" ]]; then
    35	  fail "container ${id:0:12} is labelled '$label', not $RUN_ID; not removing it"
    36	elif docker rm -f "$id" >/dev/null && ! docker inspect "$id" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    37	  echo "removed container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12})"
    38	else
    39	  fail "could not remove container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12})"
    40	fi
    41	
    42	mkdir -p "$EVIDENCE/logs" || fail "cannot create $EVIDENCE/logs"
    43	for log in "$STATE"/*.log; do
    44	  [[ -f $log ]] || continue
    45	  verify_redact < "$log" > "$EVIDENCE/logs/$(basename "$log" .log).log.txt" || fail "could not copy $log"
    46	done
    47	for file in revision.txt worktree.txt; do
    48	  [[ -f $STATE/$file ]] && { cp "$STATE/$file" "$EVIDENCE/logs/" || fail "could not copy $file"; }
    49	done
    50	
    51	if ! leaks=$(verify_leak_scan "$EVIDENCE"); then
    52	  fail "run secrets found in evidence files (names only): ${leaks//$'\n'/ }"
    53	fi
    54	
    55	if (( failures )); then
    56	  echo "cleanup: $failures failure(s); state kept at $STATE so you can fix and rerun" >&2
    57	  exit 1
    58	fi
    59	
    60	case $STATE in
    61	  "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT"/*) rm -rf -- "$STATE" ;;
    62	esac
    63	[[ $(cat "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current" 2>/dev/null) == "$RUN_ID" ]] && rm -f "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current"
    64	echo "evidence kept: $EVIDENCE ($(find "$EVIDENCE" -type f | wc -l | tr -d ' ') files)"
