# Verify FS-Billing

FS-Billing is a call-billing system. Its user surface is the web app: a customer portal (`/`, `/login`) and an admin shell (`/admin`, `/admin/login`). Both are served by `apps/web` (Vite + React) and call `apps/api` (Fastify + Better Auth) over `/api`. Postgres stores business rows under RLS and the `auth.*` schema. Secondary surfaces:

- **HTTP API.** Driven with `scripts/api.sh`. The contract is `packages/shared/src/api/contract.ts`.
- **Auth CLIs.** `auth:seed`, `auth:unlock`, and `auth:bootstrap` in `apps/api` (`docs/phase-4-api.md`). Run them through `scripts/with-env.sh`.
- **FreeSWITCH/SIPp e2e.** `freeswitch/test/run-integration.sh`. It has no UI and the browser harness does not drive it. See [features/freeswitch-sipp.md](features/freeswitch-sipp.md).
- **Worker** (`apps/worker`). Not started by this skill. The SIPp e2e covers it.

The feature map in [features/README.md](features/README.md) is the source for what to verify and how. Read it before you drive anything.

All commands run from the repo root, with `S=.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts`.

## Isolation rules (read first)

- The user's dev Postgres container `fs-billing-postgres-1` runs on `127.0.0.1:5432`. **Never touch it or its databases.** `sql.sh` and `cleanup.sh` refuse to act on it, and every helper acts only on the container labelled `fs-billing-verify=<RUN_ID>`.
- The root `.env` points at `:5432`, and every pnpm script loads it with `--env-file-if-exists`. Node never lets a `.env` value override a variable that is already exported. `launch.sh` and `with-env.sh` export every DB, auth, and port variable, so repo commands reach the run's container. Never run `pnpm db:*`, `auth:*`, or `test:*` bare in this skill. Always prefix them with `$S/with-env.sh`.
- Do not use `apps/web/e2e` (`pnpm test:e2e`) for verification. It hard-codes ports 3000/4173 and reuses whatever server is already there.
- Every run gets its own container, free ports, secrets, and evidence dir, so two runs can coexist. The SIPp e2e is the exception: it uses fixed host-network ports.

## Launch

```sh
S=.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts
$S/launch.sh            # about 10 s; the last line printed is the RUN_ID
```

From an empty database, `launch.sh` does the following:

1. It picks a `RUN_ID` (`YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ-xxxxxx`) and creates the state dir `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/` (mode 700). It records the run as current in `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/current`.
2. It writes throwaway secrets (owner/app/auth DB passwords, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD`) to `run.env` (mode 600). It never prints them.
3. It starts `docker run postgres:17.9-alpine` as `fsb-verify-<RUN_ID>`, with label `fs-billing-verify=<RUN_ID>`, a free `127.0.0.1` port, and tmpfs data. It waits until TCP `pg_isready` answers, which happens only after image init finishes.
4. It runs migrate, seed, and migrate again to check idempotence (`packages/db/src/bin/*.ts` via `tsx`), then `apps/api/scripts/seed-auth.ts` (`auth:seed`). Every seeded account gets `SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD`.
5. It starts the API (`node --import tsx src/main.ts`) under `setsid` on a free port. `BETTER_AUTH_URL` is set to the API origin, and `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` to the API and web origins.
6. It runs `vite build --outDir <state>/web-dist`, so `apps/web/dist` is never touched. Then it starts `vite preview --strictPort` under `setsid` on a free port, with `FS_BILLING_API_ORIGIN` set so `/api` proxies to this run's API.
7. **Ready** means a real `POST /api/auth/login` as `bjh003@example.com` through the web origin returns 200 with `"kind":"customer"` and `"email":"bjh003@example.com"`, and the logout that follows returns 204. Both transcripts are saved to `docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/launch/ready-login.http`. This is the stack's only login check outside the journeys.

It prints `READY` with the web, API, and Postgres addresses. If it fails, it prints the cleanup command to run for that `RUN_ID`. Prerequisites: Docker, Node 22/24, pnpm 10.34.7, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` already done, Playwright Chromium (`pnpm --filter @fs-billing/web exec playwright install chromium` if missing), `ss`, and `python3`.

To work on an older run, set `VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id>` in front of any helper. Otherwise the helpers use the run in `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/current`.

## Doctor

```sh
$S/doctor.sh            # exit 0 = healthy, 1 = at least one FAIL line
```

Doctor prints one `OK` or `FAIL` line per check:

- The container is running and is ours: id, label, and port all match. If it is not, doctor does not query the database at all.
- The API and web PIDs are alive and carry `VERIFY_RUN_ID=<RUN_ID>` in `/proc/<pid>/environ`. The listener on each port belongs to that process group.
- The git HEAD equals the revision recorded at launch. If HEAD moved, relaunch.
- Applied migrations equal the entries in `packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json`. Tenants and credentials exist. The query goes through `sql.sh`, which repeats the container guards.
- `/api/health` returns `{"status":"ok","service":"api"}`.
- The web serves the built `index.html`, and `/api` proxies to our API.

Doctor is read-only. It sends no login, writes no row, and creates no file, so it is safe to run at any time and never uses up the credential rate limit. Launch's ready check is the login proof. Run doctor first, and again whenever something looks wrong. Do not drive a run that fails doctor.

## Drive

The browser harness is Playwright (`@playwright/test` 1.64 from `apps/web` devDependencies). `drive.mjs` resolves it from there:

```sh
$S/drive.mjs customer-login          # or: admin-login login-errors session-expiry cross-tenant logout theme-and-mobile not-found
$S/drive.mjs customer-login --headed # watch it
$S/drive.mjs all                     # every feature in order, one child process each, then a summary table
```

Before it opens a browser, each journey waits until 61 s have passed since the run's last credential request (see [Gotchas](#gotchas)). It prints `waiting N s for the API's credential rate-limit window (30/min/IP) to reset` when it does. Expect `all` to spend up to a minute per feature on these waits.

Each journey runs headless Chromium (`locale zh-CN`, `Asia/Shanghai`) against the run's web origin. It prints a step table and exits `0` on PASS, `1` on FAIL, or `2` on BLOCKED (precondition missing; the reason is printed). Journeys use only ARIA roles and names taken from `apps/web/src`:

| UI | Handle |
| --- | --- |
| Login form | `getByLabel('登录邮箱')`, `getByLabel('密码', { exact: true })`, `getByRole('button', { name: '登录', exact: true })` |
| Login headings | portal `heading '登录'`, admin `heading '登录管理后台'`; admin page also shows `客户请使用客户门户登录` |
| Login problems | `getByRole('alert')`: `登录邮箱或密码错误` / `请检查后重新输入`; `登录失败次数过多，已暂时锁定` + `请在 M分SS秒 后重试` (also the disabled submit button's name); `该账号已停用` / `如需恢复使用，请联系客服` |
| Expired banner | `getByRole('status')`: `登录已过期，请重新登录` · `登录后将返回之前的页面` (on `?reason=expired`) |
| Expired dialog | `getByRole('dialog', { name: '登录已过期，请重新登录' })` → `button '重新登录'` |
| Portal home | `heading '首页'`, crumb text `ABC001 · 深圳市某某科技有限公司`, `getByRole('region', { name: '当前余额' })` → `-¥35.20` |
| Admin home | `heading '概览'`, text `欢迎，王敏。…`, `html[data-product=admin]` |
| Shell (desktop) | `button '主题'` (◐) → `menuitemradio '浅色' / '深色' / '跟随系统'`; `button '账号菜单'` → `menu` → `menuitem '退出登录'`; `navigation '主导航'` |
| Shell (mobile < 768px) | `button '打开导航'` → `dialog '导航'`; `button '账号菜单'` → `dialog '账号菜单'` with `radiogroup '主题'` and `button '退出登录'` |
| 404 | `heading '找不到这个页面'`, `link '返回首页'`; admin, in the shell: `返回首页即回到「概览」` |

Theme state is `html[data-theme]` plus `localStorage['fs-billing.theme']`. The zone is `html[data-product]`.

HTTP goes through the web origin's `/api` proxy, the same path the browser uses. `api.sh` sends `Origin: <web origin>`, which the API requires on POST:

```sh
$S/api.sh login abc001@example.com a            # seeded password, cookie jar "a"
$S/api.sh login abc001@example.com x --wrong    # fixed wrong password
$S/api.sh GET /api/portal/context a
$S/api.sh POST /api/auth/logout a
$S/api.sh POST /api/admin/portal-accounts/<uuid>/reset-password a
VERIFY_API_BASE=api $S/api.sh GET /api/health  # hit the API origin directly
```

Database access always goes into OUR container through `docker exec`, as the owner role:

```sh
$S/sql.sh "SELECT email_key, failed_count, locked_until FROM auth.login_failures"
$S/sql.sh --value "SELECT count(*) FROM auth.session"   # bare value, for scripts
$S/sql.sh < some.sql
```

Repo CLIs run with the run's environment through `with-env.sh`, which also redacts their output:

```sh
$S/with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:unlock hzx014@example.com   # emergency unlock
$S/with-env.sh pnpm test:idor                                                # IDOR suite, scratch fs_billing_test DB in our container
$S/with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:bootstrap --kind portal --profile <portal-profile-uuid> --replace < /path/to/password-file
```

`auth:bootstrap --generate` prints a new password once, and `with-env.sh` cannot redact a value it does not know. If you use `--generate`, redirect its output into the state dir, not into evidence.

Seeded accounts (from `packages/db/src/seed.ts`; all use `SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD`):

| Account | Role in journeys |
| --- | --- |
| `abc001@example.com` | ABC001, CNY, balance −35.20: main customer |
| `glb009@example.com` | GLB009, USD, −15.02: second tenant (B) |
| `hzx014@example.com` | HZX014: lockout target |
| `cdt018@example.com` | CDT018: tenant `disabled` |
| `wangmin@example.com` | admin 王敏 |
| `lijun@example.com` | admin 李俊: session revocation |
| `bjh003@example.com` | reserved for launch and doctor |

## Evidence

Evidence goes to `docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/<feature-id>/`. Cleanup never deletes it.

- `steps.md` and `result.json`: each step's action, PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED, and the observed detail.
- `sNN-<step>-before` and `sNN-<step>-after` (`.png` full-page screenshot and `.aria.yml` Playwright `ariaSnapshot`): taken automatically around every step, of the page the journey last opened or acted on. A step that drives only HTTP has no page to capture; its proof is the `.http` transcript.
- `NN-name.png` and `NN-name.aria.yml`: named captures of the states a feature file cites, such as `05-locked`.
- `browser-http.txt`: every `/api/*` response the browser saw, with the request body, status, and JSON body. Credential fields are `[REDACTED]`. `NN-name.http` holds the curl transcripts from `api.sh`.
- `NN-name.sql.txt`: the query and its rows from our container. `NN-name.cli.txt`: CLI commands and their output.
- `launch/ready-login.http` and, after cleanup, `logs/` (`*.log.txt`: api, web, build, db-setup, and auth-seed logs, redacted) plus `revision.txt`.

No evidence file ends in `.log`, because the repo's `.gitignore` ignores `*.log`. Every file the helpers write is committable as is: `git add docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>` picks up all of it. Check with `git status --ignored --short docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>`, which must list no `!!` entries.

Every file written into evidence passes through redaction. Secret values from `run.env` become `[REDACTED]`, and so do `session_token=` cookie values and the JSON fields `password`, `currentPassword`, `newPassword`, `temporaryPassword`, `token`, and `secret`. The JSON rule covers values the run never knew, such as the temporary password a real reset returns. It matches across lines, so pretty-printed JSON with the key, colon, and value on separate lines is redacted too, and it handles escaped quotes inside the value. Never paste `run.env` or a raw cookie into evidence by hand.

After you change `verify_redact` in `lib.sh`, run `$S/selftest-redact.sh`. It needs no stack. It feeds synthetic single-line, multi-line, and escaped-quote cases through the redaction and exits 1 if any value survives or any output differs from the expected text.

`cleanup.sh` scans the evidence for every `run.env` secret before it deletes the state, and fails, keeping the state, if any file matches. It prints file names only. To scan by hand while the run is live, use `-l` so a hit shows the file and never the secret:

```sh
grep -rlF -e "$(grep ^SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD= /tmp/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/run.env | cut -d= -f2)" docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>   # must print nothing
```

Proof standards:

- **Real user path.** Use the login form, menus, and links in the built app, through the same `/api` proxy. No test-only endpoints, internal setters, or seeded cookies. The API has no test hooks, and the raw Better Auth router is not mounted.
- **Action and resulting state.** Capture the screen before and after, plus the HTTP status and error `code` behind what the UI shows.
- **Side effects in rows.** Check `auth.session` (created on login, deleted on logout and revocation, none kept for disabled accounts), `auth.login_failures` (`failed_count`, `locked_until`, deleted by unlock or a successful login), `auth.account.updated_at` (unchanged by a forbidden reset), `operation_logs`, and `tenant_balances`.
- **DB edits stand in for elapsed time or an operator action.** Setting `auth.session.expires_at` into the past stands in for seven days passing. Deleting session rows is what an admin reset or `--replace` bootstrap does. Each such edit is saved as its own `.sql.txt`, and every journey says so. Never edit DB rows to make an assertion pass.
- **Mocks only at real production boundaries.** This skill mocks nothing. React Query's tab-refocus refetch is triggered with a `visibilitychange` event, the only synthetic browser event in use. It stands in for the user switching tabs, which headless Chromium cannot do.
- **Dry runs.** None of these flows has a dry-run mode. `auth:unlock` really deletes the counter row, and `auth:bootstrap --replace` really revokes sessions. Before you trust any `--dry-run` or "safe" mode added later, prove what it skips with `sql.sh` before and after.
- **Blocked is not passed.** If a precondition is missing (an account locked, seed changed), the journey exits 2 with the reason. Report it as BLOCKED, and never edit the journey to get around it.

## Cleanup

```sh
$S/cleanup.sh                           # current run
VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> $S/cleanup.sh        # a specific run
```

`cleanup.sh` does the following:

- It sends TERM, then KILL after 5 s, to the API and web process groups recorded in `api.pid` and `web.pid`. It does this only while each PID still carries this run's `VERIFY_RUN_ID`. It never kills by name or port. A group that survives KILL is a failure.
- It runs `docker rm -f` on the recorded container id, and only if the id carries label `fs-billing-verify=<RUN_ID>`, then confirms the container is gone. A container is treated as gone only when `docker inspect` reports `No such object`. Any other inspect error, such as the daemon being down or permission denied, is a failure, because the container may still exist. A failed removal, or an id with another label, is also a failure.
- It copies redacted logs to `<evidence>/logs/` as `*.log.txt`, then runs the leak scan above.
- Only if every step succeeded does it delete `/tmp/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/` (secrets, cookie jars, web build) and print `evidence kept: <dir> (<n> files)`. Otherwise it prints each failure, keeps the state so you can fix the cause and rerun it, and exits 1.

Run cleanup after every run, including failed launches. Confirm with `ls docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/<RUN_ID>/` and `docker ps --filter label=fs-billing-verify`.

Commit evidence only when a PR needs the proof. Every file is committable (see [Evidence](#evidence)).

## Helpers

| Script | Invocation | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `launch.sh` | `$S/launch.sh` | Isolated stack from an empty DB; prints RUN_ID |
| `doctor.sh` | `$S/doctor.sh` | Read-only health and ownership check (no login, no writes) |
| `drive.mjs` | `$S/drive.mjs <feature\|all> [--headed]` | Playwright journey and evidence for one feature, or all in turn |
| `api.sh` | `$S/api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]` / `$S/api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json]` | Redacted HTTP transcript via the web proxy |
| `sql.sh` | `$S/sql.sh [--value] "<sql>"` / `$S/sql.sh < file.sql` | psql as owner in OUR container only |
| `with-env.sh` | `$S/with-env.sh <command...>` | Repo CLI with the run's env, output redacted |
| `selftest-redact.sh` | `$S/selftest-redact.sh` | Offline self-test of `verify_redact`; exit 1 on any leak |
| `cleanup.sh` | `$S/cleanup.sh` | Kill our process groups, remove our container, keep evidence; exit 1 and keep state on any failure |
| `lib.sh` | sourced | Run resolution, redaction, leak scan, ownership checks |

Typical loop:

```sh
S=.cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts
RUN_ID=$($S/launch.sh | tail -n1)
$S/doctor.sh
$S/drive.mjs customer-login    # or: $S/drive.mjs all
$S/cleanup.sh
ls docs/evidence/verify-fs-billing/$RUN_ID/customer-login
```

## Gotchas

- **Credential rate limit.** `POST /api/auth/login` and `/api/auth/change-password` allow 30 requests per minute per client IP, in a fixed window that opens at the first request. Every browser and `api.sh` request in a run comes from `127.0.0.1`, so they all share one budget. Running features back to back used to hit it: `not-found` got `429 RATE_LIMITED` right after `login-errors`. Each journey now waits until 61 s have passed since the run's last credential request, which `api.sh` and `drive.mjs` record in the state dir. If you send logins by hand between journeys, they count too, and the next journey waits for them. Never raise or disable the API's limit to make a run pass. A `429 RATE_LIMITED` in a journey means the wait was bypassed; wait a minute and rerun that feature.
- **Doctor is read-only on purpose.** Do not add a login to it. A login uses up the rate-limit budget and writes a session row. Launch's ready check already proves login and logout.

Keep the map honest as the app grows: run `/maintain-verification-skill`.
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Is this run's instance worth driving? Prints one OK/FAIL line per check; exit 1 on any FAIL.
# Usage: doctor.sh            (VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> doctor.sh for a specific run)
# Read-only: no logins, no DB writes, no files. Launch's ready check is the login proof.
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load
set +e

failures=0
ok() { printf 'OK    %s\n' "$*"; }
bad() { printf 'FAIL  %s\n' "$*"; failures=$((failures + 1)); }
echo "run $RUN_ID"

# Container: ours by name, id, label, and port; never the user's.
id=$(cat "$STATE/container.id" 2>/dev/null)
read -r running label port < <(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}} {{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}} {{(index (index .NetworkSettings.Ports "5432/tcp") 0).HostPort}}' "$id" 2>/dev/null)
container_ok=false
if [[ $running == true && $label == "$RUN_ID" && $port == "$VERIFY_PG_PORT" && $VERIFY_CONTAINER != "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]]; then
  container_ok=true
  ok "postgres container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12}) running, labelled, 127.0.0.1:$port"
else
  bad "postgres container $VERIFY_CONTAINER running=$running label=$label port=$port"
fi

# Processes: alive, carry our VERIFY_RUN_ID, and own their listening port.
check_proc() {
  local name=$1 pidfile=$2 port=$3 pid listeners owned=false l
  pid=$(cat "$pidfile" 2>/dev/null)
  if [[ -z $pid ]] || ! verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then bad "$name pid ${pid:-?} not alive or not from run $RUN_ID"; return; fi
  listeners=$(verify_port_pids "$port")
  for l in $listeners; do
    [[ $(ps -o pgid= -p "$l" | tr -d ' ') == "$pid" ]] && verify_owns_pid "$l" && owned=true
  done
  if $owned; then ok "$name pid $pid alive, owns 127.0.0.1:$port"; else bad "$name port $port listeners [${listeners//$'\n'/ }] not in pgid $pid"; fi
}
check_proc api "$STATE/api.pid" "$VERIFY_API_PORT"
check_proc web "$STATE/web.pid" "$VERIFY_WEB_PORT"

# Revision: what was launched vs what is checked out now.
launched=$(cat "$STATE/revision.txt") head=$(git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" rev-parse HEAD)
dirty=$(git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" status --short | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
if [[ $launched == "$head" ]]; then ok "git $head ($dirty uncommitted paths)"; else bad "launched at $launched but HEAD is now $head; relaunch"; fi

# Database: reachable, every journal migration applied, seed and auth seed present.
# Only through sql.sh's guards, and only when the container check above passed.
expected=$(python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(len(json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))["entries"]))' "$VERIFY_REPO/packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json")
if ! $container_ok; then
  bad 'db: not queried, the container is not ours'
else
  row=$("$(dirname "$0")/sql.sh" --value \
    "SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM drizzle.__drizzle_migrations), (SELECT count(*) FROM tenants), (SELECT count(*) FROM auth.account WHERE provider_id='credential')" 2>&1)
  IFS='|' read -r applied tenants creds <<< "$row"
  if [[ $applied == "$expected" && $tenants -gt 0 && $creds -gt 0 ]]; then
    ok "db migrations $applied/$expected, tenants $tenants, credentials $creds"
  else
    bad "db: migrations ${applied:-?}/$expected tenants ${tenants:-?} credentials ${creds:-?} ($row)"
  fi
fi

# API and web.
health=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN/api/health")
[[ $health == '{"status":"ok","service":"api"}' ]] && ok "api health $health" || bad "api health: ${health:-no answer}"
html=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN/login")
[[ $html == *'<div id="root">'* && $html == *'/assets/'* ]] && ok "web serves built app at $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" || bad 'web did not serve the built index.html'
proxied=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN/api/health")
[[ $proxied == "$health" && -n $health ]] && ok 'web proxies /api to our API' || bad "web /api proxy: ${proxied:-no answer}"

if (( failures )); then echo "doctor: $failures check(s) failed"; exit 1; fi
echo 'doctor: healthy'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# HTTP against this run's stack, through the web origin's /api proxy (the browser's path).
# Usage:
#   api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]     POST /api/auth/login with the seeded password
#                                            (or a fixed wrong one); cookies go to jar <jar>
#   api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body] any other request, optionally with jar cookies
# Prints a redacted HTTP transcript (request line, response status, headers, body).
# Set VERIFY_API_BASE=api to hit the API origin directly instead of the web proxy.
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load

base=$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN
[[ ${VERIFY_API_BASE:-web} == api ]] && base=$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN
mkdir -p "$STATE/jars"

body_file=
cleanup_body() { [[ -n $body_file ]] && rm -f "$body_file"; return 0; }
trap cleanup_body EXIT

if [[ ${1:-} == login ]]; then
  [[ $# -ge 3 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]'
  email=$2 jar=$3 method=POST path=/api/auth/login
  body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
  VERIFY_EMAIL=$email VERIFY_WRONG=${4:-} python3 -c '
import json, os
pw = "wrong-password-verify" if os.environ["VERIFY_WRONG"] == "--wrong" else os.environ["SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD"]
print(json.dumps({"email": os.environ["VERIFY_EMAIL"], "password": pw}), end="")' > "$body_file"
  shown_body="{\"email\":\"$email\",\"password\":\"[REDACTED]\"}"
else
  [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body]'
  method=$1 path=$2 jar=${3:-} shown_body=${4:-}
  if [[ -n ${4:-} ]]; then
    body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
    printf '%s' "$4" > "$body_file"
  fi
fi

case $path in /api/auth/login|/api/auth/change-password) verify_note_credential_request ;; esac

args=(-sS -i -X "$method" -H "Origin: $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" --max-time 20)
[[ -n $jar ]] && args+=(-b "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt" -c "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt")
[[ -n $body_file ]] && args+=(-H 'Content-Type: application/json' --data-binary "@$body_file")

{
  printf '> %s %s%s\n' "$method" "$base" "$path"
  printf '> Origin: %s\n' "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN"
  [[ -n $jar ]] && printf '> Cookie: (jar %s)\n' "$jar"
  [[ -n $shown_body ]] && printf '> Body: %s\n' "$shown_body"
  printf '\n'
  curl "${args[@]}" "$base$path"
  printf '\n'
} | tr -d '\r' | verify_redact
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run SQL as the owner role inside THIS run's Postgres container (never the user's).
# Usage: sql.sh "SELECT ..."      or      sql.sh < file.sql
#        sql.sh --value "SELECT ..."   unaligned, tuples only (one value per line)
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load

[[ $VERIFY_CONTAINER != "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]] || die 'refusing to touch the user container'
[[ $(docker inspect -f '{{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}}' "$VERIFY_CONTAINER" 2>/dev/null) == "$RUN_ID" ]] \
  || die "container $VERIFY_CONTAINER is not labelled for run $RUN_ID"

psql=(docker exec -i "$VERIFY_CONTAINER" psql -U fs_billing -d fs_billing -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -P pager=off)
if [[ ${1:-} == --value ]]; then psql+=(-Atq); shift; fi
if [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; then
  "${psql[@]}" -c "$*"
else
  "${psql[@]}"
fi
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run a repo command with this run's environment (our DB URLs, auth secret, seed password).
# Exported values win over the root .env, which every pnpm script loads with
# --env-file-if-exists, so documented commands hit our container, not :5432.
# Usage: with-env.sh <command...>     e.g. with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:unlock hzx014@example.com
# Output is redacted; secrets never reach the terminal.
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load
[[ $# -gt 0 ]] || die 'usage: with-env.sh <command...>'
cd "$VERIFY_REPO"
set +e
"$@" 2>&1 | verify_redact | sed -E 's#(postgres(ql)?://[^:/@]+:)[^@]+@#\1[REDACTED]@#g'
exit "${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Start an isolated FS-Billing stack: dedicated Postgres container, API, web preview.
# Usage: launch.sh            (prints RUN_ID on the last line)
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"

command -v docker >/dev/null || die 'docker is required'
command -v ss >/dev/null || die 'ss (iproute2) is required for port ownership checks'
[[ -d $VERIFY_REPO/node_modules/.pnpm ]] || die 'run pnpm install --frozen-lockfile first'

RUN_ID=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)-$(python3 -c 'import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(3))')
STATE=$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/$RUN_ID
EVIDENCE=$VERIFY_EVIDENCE_ROOT/$RUN_ID
umask 077
mkdir -p "$STATE" "$EVIDENCE/launch"
chmod 700 "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT" "$STATE"
printf '%s\n' "$RUN_ID" > "$VERIFY_STATE_ROOT/current"
trap 'status=$?; (( status == 0 )) || echo "launch failed; logs in $STATE; tear down with: VERIFY_RUN_ID=$RUN_ID $(dirname "$0")/cleanup.sh" >&2' EXIT

container=fsb-verify-$RUN_ID
pg_port=$(verify_free_port)
api_port=$(verify_free_port)
web_port=$(verify_free_port)
while [[ $api_port == "$pg_port" ]]; do api_port=$(verify_free_port); done
while [[ $web_port == "$pg_port" || $web_port == "$api_port" ]]; do web_port=$(verify_free_port); done
secret() { python3 -c 'import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(24))'; }
api_origin=http://127.0.0.1:$api_port
web_origin=http://127.0.0.1:$web_port

# Every database/auth/port variable the root .env defines is set here, so even a
# command that loads .env cannot fall through to the user's database on :5432.
pg_owner=$(secret) pg_app=$(secret) pg_auth=$(secret)
cat > "$STATE/run.env" <<EOF
VERIFY_RUN_ID=$RUN_ID
VERIFY_CONTAINER=$container
VERIFY_PG_PORT=$pg_port
VERIFY_API_PORT=$api_port
VERIFY_WEB_PORT=$web_port
VERIFY_API_ORIGIN=$api_origin
VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN=$web_origin
PG_OWNER_PASSWORD=$pg_owner
PG_APP_PASSWORD=$pg_app
PG_AUTH_PASSWORD=$pg_auth
POSTGRES_DB=fs_billing
POSTGRES_USER=fs_billing
POSTGRES_PASSWORD=$pg_owner
POSTGRES_PORT=$pg_port
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://fs_billing:$pg_owner@127.0.0.1:$pg_port/fs_billing
APP_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://fs_billing_app:$pg_app@127.0.0.1:$pg_port/fs_billing
AUTH_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://fs_billing_auth:$pg_auth@127.0.0.1:$pg_port/fs_billing
API_HOST=127.0.0.1
API_PORT=$api_port
BETTER_AUTH_SECRET=$(secret)
BETTER_AUTH_URL=$api_origin
BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS=$api_origin,$web_origin
SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD=$(secret)
FS_BILLING_API_ORIGIN=$api_origin
NODE_ENV=development
EOF
chmod 600 "$STATE/run.env"
set -a; source "$STATE/run.env"; set +a
export RUN_ID STATE EVIDENCE
git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" rev-parse HEAD > "$STATE/revision.txt"
git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" status --short > "$STATE/worktree.txt"

echo "[1/6] Postgres container $container on 127.0.0.1:$pg_port"
docker run -d --name "$container" --label "fs-billing-verify=$RUN_ID" \
  -e POSTGRES_DB -e POSTGRES_USER -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD -e TZ=UTC -e PGTZ=UTC \
  -p "127.0.0.1:$pg_port:5432" --tmpfs /var/lib/postgresql/data \
  postgres:17.9-alpine > "$STATE/container.id"
# The image's init server listens on the socket only; TCP answers once init is done.
for _ in $(seq 1 120); do
  docker exec "$container" pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U fs_billing -d fs_billing >/dev/null 2>&1 && break
  sleep 0.5
done
docker exec "$container" pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U fs_billing -d fs_billing >/dev/null

# tsx is called directly (not the pnpm scripts) so the root .env is never loaded.
echo '[2/6] migrate + seed + migrate (idempotence) + auth:seed'
(
  cd "$VERIFY_REPO/packages/db"
  pnpm exec tsx src/bin/migrate.ts
  pnpm exec tsx src/bin/seed.ts
  pnpm exec tsx src/bin/migrate.ts
) > "$STATE/db-setup.log" 2>&1
(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/api" && node --import tsx scripts/seed-auth.ts) > "$STATE/auth-seed.log" 2>&1

echo "[3/6] API on $api_origin"
(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/api" && exec setsid node --import tsx src/main.ts) > "$STATE/api.log" 2>&1 &
echo $! > "$STATE/api.pid"
for _ in $(seq 1 120); do
  curl -sf "$api_origin/api/health" >/dev/null 2>&1 && break
  kill -0 "$(cat "$STATE/api.pid")" 2>/dev/null || die "API exited; see $STATE/api.log"
  sleep 0.5
done
curl -sf "$api_origin/api/health" >/dev/null

echo "[4/6] web build into $STATE/web-dist"
(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/web" && pnpm exec vite build --outDir "$STATE/web-dist" --emptyOutDir) > "$STATE/web-build.log" 2>&1

echo "[5/6] web preview on $web_origin (proxies /api to $api_origin)"
(cd "$VERIFY_REPO/apps/web" && exec setsid node_modules/.bin/vite preview --host 127.0.0.1 --port "$web_port" --strictPort --outDir "$STATE/web-dist") > "$STATE/web.log" 2>&1 &
echo $! > "$STATE/web.pid"
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
  curl -sf "$web_origin/" >/dev/null 2>&1 && break
  kill -0 "$(cat "$STATE/web.pid")" 2>/dev/null || die "web exited; see $STATE/web.log"
  sleep 0.5
done

echo '[6/6] ready check: real seeded login and logout through the web origin'
ready=$EVIDENCE/launch/ready-login.http
"$(dirname "$0")/api.sh" login bjh003@example.com ready > "$ready"
grep -q '^HTTP/1.1 200' "$ready" && grep -q '"kind":"customer"' "$ready" && grep -q '"email":"bjh003@example.com"' "$ready" \
  || die "ready login is not 200 customer bjh003@example.com; see $ready"
"$(dirname "$0")/api.sh" POST /api/auth/logout ready > "$STATE/ready-logout.http"
cat "$STATE/ready-logout.http" >> "$ready"
grep -q '^HTTP/1.1 204' "$STATE/ready-logout.http" || die "ready logout is not 204; see $ready"
rm -f "$STATE/ready-logout.http" "$STATE/jars/ready.txt"

    90	async function capture(name) {

    97	async function step(id, action, fn) {
    98	  const started = Date.now();
    99	  const prefix = `s${String(steps.length + 1).padStart(2, '0')}-${id}`;
   100	  await capture(`${prefix}-before`);
   101	  try {
   102	    const detail = await fn();
   103	    steps.push({ id, action, result: 'PASS', detail: detail ?? '', ms: Date.now() - started });
   104	  } catch (error) {
   105	    steps.push({ id, action, result: error instanceof Precondition ? 'BLOCKED' : 'FAIL', detail: String(error?.message ?? error).split('\n').slice(0, 6).join(' / '), ms: Date.now() - started });
   106	    throw error;
   107	  } finally {
   108	    await capture(`${prefix}-after`);
   109	  }
   110	}

---
name: principle-prove-it-works
description: "Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'"
user-invocable: false
---

# Prove It Works

Verify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or "it compiles."

**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.

Check the real thing, not a proxy:
- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state
- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation
- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system

Verify the process as well as the outcome. A correct result can rest on a broken process, and a review that checks results passes it: a clause reconstructed from the user's paste instead of the durable record, a constraint honored by chance from a file never read. For each fact you relied on, name the record it came from and confirm that record is the one the project's rules point at.

Red is a colour, not a measurement. A failing check proves the instrument only when the failure content is the disagreement you predicted. An exception, an empty collection against a non-empty literal, and a real mismatch all print red, so quote the assertion's diff (`Extra items in the right set`), never the assertion (`assert {...} == {...}`). A convergence probe keys on behavior only the new artifact can produce, never an identity field the old one also emits. A same-SHA restart lets old code report the new commit SHA.

## Script the check when you can

The strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.

Keep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).

   288	    const lock = await newPage();
   289	    await step('lockout', `5 wrong passwords for ${lockTarget} lock the account`, async () => {
   290	      await lock.goto('/login');
   291	      const seen = [];
   292	      for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= 5; attempt += 1) {
   293	        const r = await submitLogin(lock, lockTarget, `wrong-password-${attempt}`);
   294	        const body = await r.json();
   295	        seen.push(`${attempt}:${r.status()} ${body.error.code}${body.error.retryAfterSeconds ? ` retry=${body.error.retryAfterSeconds}` : ''}`);
   296	        if (attempt < 5) {
   297	          expect([r.status(), body.error.code]).toEqual([401, 'INVALID_CREDENTIALS']);
   298	          await expect(lock.getByRole('button', { name: '登录', exact: true })).toBeEnabled();
   299	        } else {
   300	          expect([r.status(), body.error.code]).toEqual([429, 'ACCOUNT_LOCKED']);
   301	          expect(body.error.retryAfterSeconds).toBeGreaterThan(850);
   302	        }
   303	      }
   304	      const alert = lock.getByRole('alert');
   305	      await expect(alert).toContainText('登录失败次数过多，已暂时锁定');
   306	      await expect(alert).toContainText(/请在 1[45]分\d{2}秒 后重试/);
   307	      const wait = lock.getByRole('button', { name: /^请在 \d+分\d{2}秒 后重试$/ });
   308	      await expect(wait).toBeDisabled();
   309	      await expect(lock.getByLabel('登录邮箱')).toBeDisabled();
   310	      const first = await wait.textContent();
   311	      await lock.waitForTimeout(2200);
   312	      const second = await wait.textContent();
   313	      expect(second).not.toBe(first);
   314	      await aria(lock, '05-locked');
   315	      await shot(lock, '05-locked');
   316	      const row = sql('06-lock-counter', `SELECT email_key, failed_count, locked_until > now() + interval '14 minutes' AS locked_15m FROM auth.login_failures WHERE email_key='${lockTarget}'`);
   317	      expect(row).toMatch(/hzx014@example\.com \|\s+5 \| t/);
   318	      return `${seen.join(', ')}; countdown "${first}" -> "${second}"; counter failed_count=5`;
   319	    });
   320	    await step('locked-correct-password', 'correct password while locked is still refused', async () => {
   321	      const r = api('07-locked-correct-password', ['login', lockTarget, 'locked']);
   322	      expect([r.status, r.body?.error?.code]).toEqual([429, 'ACCOUNT_LOCKED']);
   323	      return `429 ACCOUNT_LOCKED retryAfterSeconds=${r.body.error.retryAfterSeconds}`;
   324	    });
   325	    await step('emergency-unlock', 'owner runs auth:unlock; counter row deleted; login works', async () => {
   326	      const output = cli('08-auth-unlock', ['pnpm', '--filter', '@fs-billing/api', 'auth:unlock', lockTarget]);
   327	      expect(output).toContain('解除锁定计数 1 行');
   328	      expect(scalar(`SELECT count(*) FROM auth.login_failures WHERE email_key='${lockTarget}'`)).toBe('0');
   329	      sql('09-counter-after-unlock', `SELECT count(*) AS counter_rows FROM auth.login_failures WHERE email_key='${lockTarget}'`);
   330	      await lock.reload();
   331	      const r = await submitLogin(lock, lockTarget);
   332	      expect(r.status()).toBe(200);
   333	      await expectPath(lock, '/');
   334	      await expect(lock.getByText('HZX014 · 杭州星讯通信有限公司')).toBeVisible();
   335	      await shot(lock, '10-after-unlock');
   336	      return output.trim().split('\n').at(-1);
   337	    });
   338	    await step('disabled', 'disabled tenant cdt018 sees the disabled message, no session kept', async () => {
   339	      const page2 = await newPage();
   340	      await page2.goto('/login');
   341	      const r = await submitLogin(page2, 'cdt018@example.com');
   342	      expect([r.status(), (await r.json()).error.code]).toEqual([403, 'ACCOUNT_DISABLED']);
   343	      await expect(page2.getByRole('alert')).toContainText('该账号已停用');
   344	      await expect(page2.getByRole('alert')).toContainText('如需恢复使用，请联系客服');
   345	      await shot(page2, '11-disabled');
   346	      expect(scalar(sessionsOf('cdt018@example.com'))).toBe('0');
   347	      sql('12-disabled-state', `SELECT t.code, t.status, (SELECT count(*) FROM auth.session s JOIN auth."user" u ON u.id=s.user_id WHERE u.email='cdt018@example.com') AS sessions FROM tenants t WHERE t.code='CDT018'`);
   348	      return '403 ACCOUNT_DISABLED; 0 session rows';
   349	    });

   458	    requireUnlocked('abc001@example.com');
   459	    const page = await newPage();
   460	    let cookie;
   461	    /** Send the browser's complete signed session cookie, outside the browser. */
   462	    const replay = async (label) => {
   463	      const r = await fetch(`${web}/api/auth/session`, { headers: { cookie: `${cookie.name}=${cookie.value}` } });
   464	      const body = await r.json();
   465	      appendFileSync(join(out, httpLog), redact(`${new Date().toISOString()} replay ${label}: GET /api/auth/session (full signed cookie) -> ${r.status} ${JSON.stringify(body)}\n`));
   466	      return { status: r.status, body };
   467	    };
   468	    await step('desktop-menu', 'account menu -> 退出登录 removes the session row', async () => {
   469	      await loginAs(page, '/login', 'abc001@example.com');
   470	      await expectPath(page, '/');
   471	      cookie = (await page.context().cookies()).find((c) => c.name === 'better-auth.session_token');
   472	      const token = decodeURIComponent(cookie.value).split('.')[0];
   473	      secrets.push(token, cookie.value);
   474	      // Match on a hash so the token never appears in a command line or error message.
   475	      const sessionRows = `SELECT count(*) FROM auth.session WHERE encode(sha256(convert_to(token, 'UTF8')), 'hex') = '${createHash('sha256').update(token).digest('hex')}'`;
   476	      expect(scalar(sessionRows)).toBe('1');
   477	      const live = await replay('before logout');
   478	      expect([live.status, live.body.identity?.email]).toEqual([200, 'abc001@example.com']);
   479	      await page.getByRole('button', { name: '账号菜单' }).click();

   481	      await expect(menu).toContainText('abc001@example.com');

   485	      await expectPath(page, '/login');
   486	      expect(scalar(sessionRows)).toBe('0');
   487	      write('02-session-row.sql.txt', `-- SELECT count(*) FROM auth.session WHERE token=<this browser's session token>\nbefore logout: 1\nafter logout: 0\n`);
   488	      expect((await page.context().cookies()).some((c) => c.name === 'better-auth.session_token')).toBe(false);
   489	      await shot(page, '03-after-logout');
   490	      return 'session row for this cookie deleted; cookie cleared';
   491	    });
   492	    await step('revisit', 'revisit / after logout -> /login; the same signed cookie that worked before logout -> 401', async () => {
   493	      await page.goto('/');
   494	      await expectPath(page, '/login');
   495	      const r = await replay('after logout');
   496	      expect([r.status, r.body.error.code]).toEqual([401, 'UNAUTHENTICATED']);
   497	      return 'old cookie: 200 before logout, 401 UNAUTHENTICATED after';
   498	    });

/bin/bash -lc 'cat .cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/doctor.sh .cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/api.sh .cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/sql.sh .cursor/skills/verify-fs-billing/scripts/with-env.sh' in /workspace/fsb-verify-review
 succeeded in 0ms:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Is this run's instance worth driving? Prints one OK/FAIL line per check; exit 1 on any FAIL.
# Usage: doctor.sh            (VERIFY_RUN_ID=<id> doctor.sh for a specific run)
# Read-only: no logins, no DB writes, no files. Launch's ready check is the login proof.
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load
set +e

failures=0
ok() { printf 'OK    %s\n' "$*"; }
bad() { printf 'FAIL  %s\n' "$*"; failures=$((failures + 1)); }
echo "run $RUN_ID"

# Container: ours by name, id, label, and port; never the user's.
id=$(cat "$STATE/container.id" 2>/dev/null)
read -r running label port < <(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Running}} {{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}} {{(index (index .NetworkSettings.Ports "5432/tcp") 0).HostPort}}' "$id" 2>/dev/null)
container_ok=false
if [[ $running == true && $label == "$RUN_ID" && $port == "$VERIFY_PG_PORT" && $VERIFY_CONTAINER != "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]]; then
  container_ok=true
  ok "postgres container $VERIFY_CONTAINER (${id:0:12}) running, labelled, 127.0.0.1:$port"
else
  bad "postgres container $VERIFY_CONTAINER running=$running label=$label port=$port"
fi

# Processes: alive, carry our VERIFY_RUN_ID, and own their listening port.
check_proc() {
  local name=$1 pidfile=$2 port=$3 pid listeners owned=false l
  pid=$(cat "$pidfile" 2>/dev/null)
  if [[ -z $pid ]] || ! verify_owns_pid "$pid"; then bad "$name pid ${pid:-?} not alive or not from run $RUN_ID"; return; fi
  listeners=$(verify_port_pids "$port")
  for l in $listeners; do
    [[ $(ps -o pgid= -p "$l" | tr -d ' ') == "$pid" ]] && verify_owns_pid "$l" && owned=true
  done
  if $owned; then ok "$name pid $pid alive, owns 127.0.0.1:$port"; else bad "$name port $port listeners [${listeners//$'\n'/ }] not in pgid $pid"; fi
}
check_proc api "$STATE/api.pid" "$VERIFY_API_PORT"
check_proc web "$STATE/web.pid" "$VERIFY_WEB_PORT"

# Revision: what was launched vs what is checked out now.
launched=$(cat "$STATE/revision.txt") head=$(git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" rev-parse HEAD)
dirty=$(git -C "$VERIFY_REPO" status --short | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
if [[ $launched == "$head" ]]; then ok "git $head ($dirty uncommitted paths)"; else bad "launched at $launched but HEAD is now $head; relaunch"; fi

# Database: reachable, every journal migration applied, seed and auth seed present.
# Only through sql.sh's guards, and only when the container check above passed.
expected=$(python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(len(json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))["entries"]))' "$VERIFY_REPO/packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json")
if ! $container_ok; then
  bad 'db: not queried, the container is not ours'
else
  row=$("$(dirname "$0")/sql.sh" --value \
    "SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM drizzle.__drizzle_migrations), (SELECT count(*) FROM tenants), (SELECT count(*) FROM auth.account WHERE provider_id='credential')" 2>&1)
  IFS='|' read -r applied tenants creds <<< "$row"
  if [[ $applied == "$expected" && $tenants -gt 0 && $creds -gt 0 ]]; then
    ok "db migrations $applied/$expected, tenants $tenants, credentials $creds"
  else
    bad "db: migrations ${applied:-?}/$expected tenants ${tenants:-?} credentials ${creds:-?} ($row)"
  fi
fi

# API and web.
health=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN/api/health")
[[ $health == '{"status":"ok","service":"api"}' ]] && ok "api health $health" || bad "api health: ${health:-no answer}"
html=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN/login")
[[ $html == *'<div id="root">'* && $html == *'/assets/'* ]] && ok "web serves built app at $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" || bad 'web did not serve the built index.html'
proxied=$(curl -sf --max-time 5 "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN/api/health")
[[ $proxied == "$health" && -n $health ]] && ok 'web proxies /api to our API' || bad "web /api proxy: ${proxied:-no answer}"

if (( failures )); then echo "doctor: $failures check(s) failed"; exit 1; fi
echo 'doctor: healthy'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# HTTP against this run's stack, through the web origin's /api proxy (the browser's path).
# Usage:
#   api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]     POST /api/auth/login with the seeded password
#                                            (or a fixed wrong one); cookies go to jar <jar>
#   api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body] any other request, optionally with jar cookies
# Prints a redacted HTTP transcript (request line, response status, headers, body).
# Set VERIFY_API_BASE=api to hit the API origin directly instead of the web proxy.
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load

base=$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN
[[ ${VERIFY_API_BASE:-web} == api ]] && base=$VERIFY_API_ORIGIN
mkdir -p "$STATE/jars"

body_file=
cleanup_body() { [[ -n $body_file ]] && rm -f "$body_file"; return 0; }
trap cleanup_body EXIT

if [[ ${1:-} == login ]]; then
  [[ $# -ge 3 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh login <email> <jar> [--wrong]'
  email=$2 jar=$3 method=POST path=/api/auth/login
  body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
  VERIFY_EMAIL=$email VERIFY_WRONG=${4:-} python3 -c '
import json, os
pw = "wrong-password-verify" if os.environ["VERIFY_WRONG"] == "--wrong" else os.environ["SEED_AUTH_PASSWORD"]
print(json.dumps({"email": os.environ["VERIFY_EMAIL"], "password": pw}), end="")' > "$body_file"
  shown_body="{\"email\":\"$email\",\"password\":\"[REDACTED]\"}"
else
  [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die 'usage: api.sh <METHOD> <PATH> [jar] [json-body]'
  method=$1 path=$2 jar=${3:-} shown_body=${4:-}
  if [[ -n ${4:-} ]]; then
    body_file=$(mktemp "$STATE/body.XXXXXX")
    printf '%s' "$4" > "$body_file"
  fi
fi

case $path in /api/auth/login|/api/auth/change-password) verify_note_credential_request ;; esac

args=(-sS -i -X "$method" -H "Origin: $VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN" --max-time 20)
[[ -n $jar ]] && args+=(-b "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt" -c "$STATE/jars/$jar.txt")
[[ -n $body_file ]] && args+=(-H 'Content-Type: application/json' --data-binary "@$body_file")

{
  printf '> %s %s%s\n' "$method" "$base" "$path"
  printf '> Origin: %s\n' "$VERIFY_WEB_ORIGIN"
  [[ -n $jar ]] && printf '> Cookie: (jar %s)\n' "$jar"
  [[ -n $shown_body ]] && printf '> Body: %s\n' "$shown_body"
  printf '\n'
  curl "${args[@]}" "$base$path"
  printf '\n'
} | tr -d '\r' | verify_redact
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run SQL as the owner role inside THIS run's Postgres container (never the user's).
# Usage: sql.sh "SELECT ..."      or      sql.sh < file.sql
#        sql.sh --value "SELECT ..."   unaligned, tuples only (one value per line)
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load

[[ $VERIFY_CONTAINER != "$VERIFY_FORBIDDEN_CONTAINER" ]] || die 'refusing to touch the user container'
[[ $(docker inspect -f '{{index .Config.Labels "fs-billing-verify"}}' "$VERIFY_CONTAINER" 2>/dev/null) == "$RUN_ID" ]] \
  || die "container $VERIFY_CONTAINER is not labelled for run $RUN_ID"

psql=(docker exec -i "$VERIFY_CONTAINER" psql -U fs_billing -d fs_billing -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -P pager=off)
if [[ ${1:-} == --value ]]; then psql+=(-Atq); shift; fi
if [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; then
  "${psql[@]}" -c "$*"
else
  "${psql[@]}"
fi
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run a repo command with this run's environment (our DB URLs, auth secret, seed password).
# Exported values win over the root .env, which every pnpm script loads with
# --env-file-if-exists, so documented commands hit our container, not :5432.
# Usage: with-env.sh <command...>     e.g. with-env.sh pnpm --filter @fs-billing/api auth:unlock hzx014@example.com
# Output is redacted; secrets never reach the terminal.
source "$(dirname "$0")/lib.sh"
verify_load
[[ $# -gt 0 ]] || die 'usage: with-env.sh <command...>'
cd "$VERIFY_REPO"
set +e
"$@" 2>&1 | verify_redact | sed -E 's#(postgres(ql)?://[^:/@]+:)[^@]+@#\1[REDACTED]@#g'
exit "${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
